Rings

The catalogue's 16th family is the rings: ten of them, made at the ring bench from three refined goods of their grade, always at ★1. They sell at the bin like any good, never in demand, and sit on the Warehouse's Rings tab while one is held. The ring bench is the romance's.

The ten rings, their grades, prices and bonuses are in the ring, the wedding, and married life.

The shipping bin

The bin takes anything with a price and pays at night. There's no cap on selling, and nothing is ever bought back.

  • Ship a crate · 1 action: pick what to ship in the Warehouse's pick mode, as many goods and stars as you like, and one action puts the whole crate in today's bin. Shipping again the same day is another crate and another action. Seeds and dishes don't sell ("That doesn't sell").
  • Ship all · 1 action: a good's sheet in the Warehouse ships every star of it as one crate.
  • Take back: a whole line from the bin back to the Warehouse, free, any time until the night. After the night, a sale is final.
  • The estimate: "About 1,240g tonight", at today's prices.
  • The bin's sheet (from the bin on the map, the gold chip or a morning line): "About 1,240g tonight", today's lines (icon, "Carrot ★2 × 3", the line's gold, In demand), each with Take back, and Ship · 1 action, which opens the Warehouse to pick a crate. Empty, it says "Empty" and "Tap Ship to pick goods. They pay tonight." Its help (i) says the rule in three lines.

At night (step 1): - each good and star pays price × stars × demand on the day it was shipped, rounded to 5g a unit, × the count; - gold rises by the total; - the farm's gold shipped rises by the same (the Farm Rank's points, 1 per 100g, and its shipping milestones); - each good's count joins the Diary's shipped goods; - the bin empties.

The morning's bin section lists each good and star sold, with its gold and In demand, then the total. A line's gold is the night's own and is never priced again.

Gold

Gold is whole and never goes below 0; a purchase short of gold is refused ("Need 30g more"). A new farm starts with 500g and 9 Carrot seeds. - Earned: the bin, and the Diary's milestones and collection rows; a loan lends toward a build. - Spent: the night's upkeep and loans' shares, seeds at the shop, places to build at their quick looks or the mayor's office, Dorm levels at the Dorm, and houses on the homestead.

The gold chip in the bar shows the coin and your gold, and opens the bin.

The shop

The shop is in town: its sheet (from the town's Shop, the Menu, or a tile's Seeds at the shop, in town) first asks you to go in, "The shop is in town." with Go into town · 1 action; once you're in town today, it's open, free, for the rest of the day. It lists the season's six seeds, each with its icon, name and price and, while shut, its gate ("From Farm Rank 2"). A tap opens a buy sheet: ×1, ×5, ×10, ×25 or a typed count, up to 99, and Buy 9 · 180g, held back with "Need 30g more" when gold is short. What you buy goes in the Warehouse at ★1. The shop checks its lines again as you buy, so a sheet left open past a season's turn can't buy last season's seeds. Beds and stations are bought at their own places.

The Warehouse

The Warehouse holds everything the farm owns, by stars: each good has five counts, ★1 to ★5. It has no limit.

  • Tabs, in a row that scrolls sideways, each with its count: Crops (the 27 crops and the grains' refined forms), Woods, Mushrooms, Herbs and spices, Fruits, Nuts and seeds, Sweets, Fish, Mining (ores, stones and crystals) and Seeds; and Loot (the expeditions' goods), Rings and Dishes only while something is on them. A crop's refined form sits on its family's tab (Dried Basil on Herbs and spices, Peanut Butter on Nuts and seeds).
  • Each good: its icon, name, In demand, its total, and its five stars, each with its count and today's price.
  • A good's sheet: its family, grade and form ("Fish · G5 · raw"), what it refines into or from, "+20% this season" when in demand, its five stars and Ship all · 665g · 1 action. Seeds say "Seeds: they don't sell"; a dish says "A dish: for a meal"; a ring's form is "made at the ring bench".
  • Pick mode (from the bin's Ship): "Ship to the bin". A tap on a star puts one in the crate (marked "+2" on the star, its count what's left), All puts every star of a good in, and a bar says "Crate: 3 · about 425g" with Clear and Ship · 1 action, which ships the crate and goes back to the bin. With nothing picked it says "Tap a star to put one in the crate." (or what's in the bin tonight). It opens on the first tab holding something that sells.

Every family's prices

Each family's goods, raw and refined, at ★1 and ★5, out of demand; in demand, ×1.2 and rounded to 5g again.

Upkeep and loans

The farm has to pay its way. What you add to it costs a little each night, and the mayor's office lends toward a build so it can stand ahead of the gold; each night's income pays it back. The rules are farm/economy.ts.

 the night, step 1:  the bin pays
 the night, step 2:  the farmhands work ──► the farm eats (food) ──► each loan takes its share of the night's income
                                                                     (a tenth of the loan, from income only)
                                                                 ──► the takeout, then the upkeep, with anything owed
                                                                                            │
                     enough gold ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤ all paid
                     short ── what gold there is pays, the rest is owed ──► the morning warns (night 1)
                                                                        ──► half pace (night 2 on), until it's paid
 the day:  Go into town · 1 ──► the mayor's office (free): Borrow 400g · Build ──► the build is ordered at once

Upkeep

Every night, after the farm eats and the loans take their share of the bin's pay, the farm pays its takeout and then its upkeep:

CountsA nightNot counted
Each place built (the stations, the Coop and the Barn, the Mine and the Quarry, the Dock, the Boat, and each later one)15gThe start’s own places; the animals, whose cost is their feed
Each house built on the homestead15gThe Farmhouse
Each Dorm bed past the first 3, at the level in effect5gLevel 1’s 3 beds
Each Uma living on the farm or working it: home in the Dorm, a spouse in her house, or a farmhand wherever she lives; once each10gThose who live in town and don’t work the farm
Short of itWhat gold there is pays; the rest is owed with tomorrow’sThe first short night warns; from night 2 in a row, half pace until it’s paid
  • What it counts is read at night step 2, before the night's builds stand: a place or a Dorm level paid for today costs from tomorrow night, its first night standing, and a house (which stands at once) from tonight. A new farm owes no upkeep: it grows with what you build and who you bring. (It pays its farmer's takeout from the first night: food.)
  • What pays: the farm's gold after the bin, the loans' shares and the night's takeout. Gold never goes below 0.
  • Short of upkeep: what gold there is pays, takeout first, and the rest is owed (farm.owed), takeout and upkeep alike, added to the next night's upkeep. The short nights in a row are counted (farm.short):
    • the first warns in the morning ("Short of upkeep · 40g owed", tagged Warning);
    • from the second, the farmhands work at half pace until it's paid ("· farmhands at half pace"): half their actions, rounded down, never below one (atPace), from the day after the second short night (her day). The farmer's own ten are never touched;
    • paid: a night whose gold covers all that's owed and its own upkeep pays it, says "Upkeep paid up", and the count goes back to 0.
    • Nobody leaves, and nothing is lost: no one moves out, no building stops, no good is taken.
  • The book keeps the bin's pay on each of the last seven nights (farm.income), so the Book can set an average night's income beside the upkeep.

Loans

The mayor's office, in town, lends toward a build. Going into town takes its action; at the office, ordering and borrowing are free.

Farm Rank12345678910
Lends up to1,000g2,000g4,000g8,000g15,000g30,000g50,000g80,000g120,000g160,000g
Loans at once1112222222
TermThe rule
What it lendsWhat the build is short of gold, rounded up to 100g, never past its cost; you bring the goods
TowardA place offered at your Farm Rank (its game built), the Dorm’s next level at its Farm Rank, or the next house once its wedding has come
The fee10% of the loan, rounded up to 10g, owed with it
Each nightA tenth of what’s owed when it’s taken (rounded up to 5g), from the night’s income only, before the upkeep
RepayAt the office, in full, any time you’re in town; the fee isn’t returned
  • The office's orders (buildOrders): every place offered at your Farm Rank whose game is built and that doesn't stand (one paid today says "Builds tonight"), the Dorm's next level once its Farm Rank comes, and the next house once its wedding has come. Each says what it costs, and:
    • Build · 1,000g when the gold and goods are there: the build as its own card orders it, with your gold;
    • Borrow 400g · Build when only gold is short and the office lends: what it's short, rounded up to 100g, with the terms under it ("Need 360g more · 440g owed, a tenth a night from income"). The loan and the build are one tap: a place stands the next morning, a Dorm level's beds come the next morning, and a house stands at once, named in its row's field as on the homestead;
    • otherwise why not: its goods ("Need 20 logs more."), the loans held ("One loan at a time: this one first.", "Two loans at a time: one of these first."), or the limit ("The office lends up to 1,000g at Farm Rank 1.").
  • Each night, after the bin pays and the farm eats, and before the takeout and the upkeep, each loan in the order taken takes its share (nightly, a tenth of what was owed when it was taken, rounded up to 5g) from what's left of the night's income; a night with no sales repays nothing, and the loan runs a night longer. A loan with nothing owed goes, and the morning says "cleared".
  • Repay: at the office, the whole of what's owed, from your gold, any time you're in town; the fee isn't returned.
  • When it clears: the nights at its share (what's owed ÷ its share, rounded up) from today: "clears in about 10 nights (Spring 14)", as the Book, the projects list and the office say it. "About", since a night with fewer sales repays less.
  • Ordering a build stays where it is. A place's Build card on the farm, the Dorm's page and the homestead still order with your own gold and goods, free and with no trip to town; the office orders the same builds, and only it lends. Paying your own way needs nobody's leave, and builds are rare, so a trip to town would add a step and no choice: the trip is the price of credit.

The save: farm.loans (each loan's project, the day taken, what was lent, the fee, what's owed and its share a night; at most two), farm.owed and farm.short (together: both 0, or both above), and farm.income (up to seven nights). They grow v5 in place: a save from before them reads as no loans, nothing owed and no nights, and nothing moves updatedAt for it.

Food

Everyone who eats on the farm eats twice a day, from the first night. What isn't cooked is bought in as takeout, at a price you feel from day one; cooking turns the Warehouse's goods into meals in batches, and a farmhand on Cook feeds many. The rules are farm/food.ts.

 your day                                      the night, step 2
   the homestead's sheet (the Farmhouse):        the farmhands work: a cook's batches into the pantry
     Kitchen · Cook a batch · 1                  the farm eats: two meals a mouth, from the pantry first,
       two goods of food → four meals              the Umas first, the farmer last, the oldest meals first
     once married: Breakfast · 1, Dinner · 1     ★2+ meal → that Uma is well fed tomorrow (+1 action)
       (each a batch too)                        what's short: takeout, 15g a meal ──► the bill, with the upkeep
   the Book: a farmhand on Cook                  meals cooked today keep for tomorrow; older ones spoil
 the morning:  From the pantry · 4 meals · Well fed      Takeout · 30g      Upkeep · 45g
The rule
Who eatsThe farmer, everyone living on the farm (home in the Dorm, a spouse in her house) and every farmhand who works that day (spends an action tonight): 2 meals each, at night
The pantryCooked meals wait here, by the day cooked and their stars. A meal keeps 2 days: cooked today, it’s eaten tonight or tomorrow night, then it spoils
The night’s mealsNight step 2, after the farmhands work: the Umas eat first (the farmhands who worked, in their order, then the rest of the farm), the farmer last; the oldest meals first, the best of those
Takeout15g a meal the pantry is short, charged with the upkeep (takeout first); a new farm’s farmer eats 2: 30g a night
Short of itTakeout never goes unpaid: what gold there is pays, the rest is owed with the upkeep, and the short nights count as the upkeep’s
Cook a batchThe farmer, in the farmhouse kitchen (the homestead’s sheet): 1 action, 4 meals from 2 goods of food you choose
A household mealBreakfast or dinner once married: the farmer cooking for the household, so it cooks a batch too (4 meals from 2 goods by the plain rule, when the Warehouse holds them)
Cook (a farmhand’s task)A batch an action from 2 goods by the plain rule, its meals by her Cooking (below), until the pantry holds tonight’s meals for everyone
A meal’s starsIts 2 goods’ stars, averaged and rounded down
Well fedAn Uma who eats a ★2+ meal is well fed through tomorrow: one more action tomorrow, and her work’s quality, hauls and rank points; never stacked, never shortening a meal running longer
The plain ruleThe two cheapest goods of ★2 or more (their meals feed well); with fewer than two held, the two cheapest. Cheapest is the price at its stars, out of demand
FoodGrains, Vegetables, Herbs and spices, Nuts and seeds, Sweets, Fruits, Mushrooms, Fish, Eggs and milk, raw or refined

A farmhand's batch by her Cooking (her aptitude, aptitude):

Cooking12345678910
Meals a batch4567889101112
Goods a batch2222222222
  • Who eats: the farmer, everyone living on the farm (home in the Dorm, or a spouse in her house), and every farmhand who works that day: one who spent an action tonight, wherever she lives. A farmhand from town with nothing to do eats at home. An Uma who lives on the farm eats whether she works or not, so a resident with no work costs her upkeep and her meals.
  • A new farm pays its farmer's two meals of takeout the first night: the first morning says "Takeout · 30g".
  • The pantry keeps meals by the day they were cooked and their stars (farm.pantry). The night eats the oldest first and the best of those, so yesterday's meals go before today's. A meal keeps two days: cooked today, it's eaten tonight or tomorrow night; any left after that spoil ("· 2 spoiled").
  • Takeout never goes unpaid: it's charged with the upkeep, takeout first; what gold can't cover is owed with the upkeep's, and the short nights count as the upkeep's (upkeep). Nobody goes hungry.
  • Cook a batch is the farmer's, in the farmhouse kitchen: the Kitchen on the homestead's sheet (a tap on the Farmhouse on the farm map, or Kitchen › in the Book). It shows the pantry, tonight's table as the farm stands ("4 at the table · 8 meals"), tonight's takeout or "The pantry covers tonight", and the goods of food held, the plain rule's two picked to start: tap to choose any two (a good held twice can be picked twice), and Cook a batch · 1 cooks four meals, "4 meals ★2 from Salmon and Salmon: well fed". Refused with fewer than two goods of food ("Two goods of food make a batch: crops, fish, eggs and milk, mushrooms, fruits, nuts or sweets.") and with no action left.
  • A household meal (married life: the household's meals) is the farmer cooking for the household, so it cooks a batch too: four meals for the pantry from two goods by the plain rule, when the Warehouse holds them; without them it's served as before. Its scene, its +10 and its well fed stay.
  • Cook is a task on the work board (the work board), with nowhere to choose: in the farmhouse kitchen, each action cooks a batch from two goods by the plain rule, its meals by her Cooking (4 at Cooking 1, 12 at Cooking 10), until the pantry holds tonight's meals for everyone (as the board stands: the farmer, everyone living on the farm and every farmhand with a task), then her other tasks take her actions. Its aptitude is Cooking, from her cooking talent; her day there pays cooking rank points (the kitchen's place), bonds with another cook, and a cooking line's +10.
  • The plain rule takes the two cheapest goods of ★2 or more, so the meals feed well; with fewer than two held, the two cheapest. Cheapest is the price at its stars, out of demand. Food is the food families' goods, raw or refined: grains, vegetables, herbs and spices, nuts and seeds, sweets, fruits, mushrooms, fish, eggs and milk (never woods, ores, stones, crystals, fibers, hides, bones or essences, a seed, a ring or a dish).
  • A meal's stars are its two goods' average, rounded down: two ★2 goods make ★2 meals, a ★2 and a ★1 make ★1. An Uma who eats a ★2+ meal is well fed through tomorrow (well fed): one more action tomorrow, and her work's quality, hauls and rank points while it lasts. The farmer gets nothing from it, so for him a ★1 meal is as good.
  • The Kitchen (Phase 2.3) adds the cooking minigame: a batch the farmer cooks there plays it, and its grade lifts the meals' stars (cookBatch's lift, 0 until then). Until then, cooking is the action.
  • The save: farm.pantry (each lot: the day cooked, today or yesterday; its stars; its meals; one lot a day and star, the oldest first). It grows v5 in place: a save from before food loads with an empty pantry, and nothing moves updatedAt for it.

What food costs, and what cooking saves

The season simulations (the pacing) play the year alone and with two farmhands, once cooking and once on takeout alone:

A yearTakeoutIts share of the binMeals from the pantryShort nightsGold at the year's end
The farmer alone, on takeout1,440g (96 meals)16%004,655g
The farmer alone, cooking for a tight night1,320g (88 meals)15%804,850g
Two farmhands, on takeout (four Umas home by Winter)7,500g (500 meals)23%0017,280g
Two farmhands, the field hand on Cook4,440g (296 meals)12%204024,605g
  • Takeout bites and never sinks a farm: alone it's a sixth of what the bin pays, and never a short night; with two farmhands and four idle Umas home it's nearly a quarter.
  • Cooking pays for itself once the field yields: alone, two goods are worth about the four meals of takeout they save, so the farmer cooks to bridge a tight night (the Mill's, on a loan) and buys the rest. With a cook, the farm feeds itself from the pantry by Autumn (takeout falls from 1,755g in Summer to 750g in Winter) and her ★2 meals keep the farmhands well fed (51 well-fed nights in Winter), so it ends the year 42% richer than on takeout.
  • At scale, the whole farm's cook (Cooking 6 by its second year) feeds eight a night from the pantry, nearly all of it ★2: its second year ships nearly twice what it did before food (256,275g against 137,600g), the well-fed farmhands' extra action and hauls doing it. F91 (work at scale) takes the farm's work and prices on from here.

Part 2 — The Umas and the farm's progress

Every character lives in the town from day 1, and you meet her there by introducing yourself, one of the day's actions. Met, she keeps living in town, where you find her for a talk, a gift and her events; Invite to the farm on her profile brings her into a free Dorm bed. The Dorm's beds cap who lives and works on the farm at once. Every Uma has a talent, G to S, in each of nine activities, read from her stats, and climbs six ranks in each by working and meeting a challenge. The farm climbs a ladder of its own, the Farm Rank, from what it earns and grows, and grows by building: the rank offers each place, and you build it with gold and the farm's goods. There are no global chapters and no farm-wide story.

 day 1: your name ──► the opening's lines ──► Home: nobody met, everyone in town
 the town: Go into town (1 action) ──► walk to her (free) ──► Introduce yourself (1 action): met ──► her meeting scene
           she lives in town ──► Invite to the farm (her profile) ── a bed free? ──► the Dorm
 the Dorm: Upgrade · 1,000g ── night ──► 5 beds (beds only)
 swap: one Uma home ⇄ one in town ── night ──► she's home

The roster

The roster is everyone you've met, and her joined the day you met her. Who is home (on the farm) is capped by the Dorm's beds; everyone else on the roster lives in town as a friend. A spouse in her house is on the farm too, without a Dorm bed. Only Umas on the farm spend evenings; a farmhand works the farm wherever she lives; talks, gifts and her events reach everyone you've met, on the farm or in town, an action each.

How Umas join

You meet her in town by introducing yourself: see meeting her. Met, she joins the roster at Friendship 0 and lives in town; Invite to the farm on her profile brings her into a free Dorm bed at once. There's no cap on how many you meet. Nothing is drawn.

The Dorm's levels and beds

Beds cap who lives and works on the farm at once. Level 1 comes with the farm; each later level is bought at the Dorm. The Dorm for 20: its eight levels, their beds, what each costs, and the Farm Rank it needs. The levels bring beds only:

LevelBedsCostsFarm RankCumulative
13——0g
251,000g11,000g
373,000g24,000g
498,000g312,000g
51115,000g427,000g
61428,000g555,000g
71750,000g6105,000g
82085,000g7190,000g
  • Buying: the gold goes now; the beds come the next morning. A purchase is refused at the top level ("The Dorm is at its top level."), before its Farm Rank ("Opens at Farm Rank 2.") and when gold is short. A level bought today can be followed by the next one the same day; both arrive the next morning.
  • Beds free are the level in effect's beds less the Umas home. A level bought today doesn't count until it takes effect.
  • Who comes home: one you've met lives in town until you invite her: Invite to the farm on her profile brings her once a bed is free (at once), or a swap the next morning.
  • Invite to the farm: one you've met who lives in town moves into a free Dorm bed now and can work today. Refused with no bed free ("No bed is free: upgrade the Dorm, or swap."), for one arriving by a swap tomorrow, and for one on the farm already.

Living in town, and swaps

Everyone you've met who isn't on the farm lives in town. She keeps her ranks and her route, and earns no rank points. Talks, gifts, her evening and her events reach her: you find her in town on her days, where her fixed schedule puts her (who's where), and ask her out there. Once she's a farmhand she's on the farm by day, in front of you every day (farmhands).

  • A swap sends one Uma home to town and brings one from town home in her place. It is free and unlimited, and it happens the next morning: the one leaving goes to town and the one arriving takes her bed.
  • Who can go to town: any Uma home. Refused: one already leaving ("She leaves tomorrow.") and anyone not home ("She isn't on the farm.").
  • Who can come home: anyone on the roster who lives in town and isn't already arriving ("She arrives tomorrow."). One home already is refused ("She's on the farm already."), and so is a spouse in her house ("She lives in her house.").
  • Leaving tomorrow: until the night, the swap picker shows an Uma a swap takes out greyed out with "Leaves tomorrow".
  • Calling off: a swap can be called off any time before the night; she stays.

The night's roster step and the morning's news

Night step 8 runs, in order, after the calendar has turned:

  1. The Dorm settles: a level bought takes effect, then each swap: the Uma leaving goes to town and the one arriving comes home in her place.
  2. The goals (see the night's goals check).

The night meets no one: you meet everyone by introducing yourself in town.

The places paid for yesterday stand first, before the Dorm settles. The morning's New today lists, after the season, festival, built, Farm Rank, milestone and household lines:

LineWhenOpens
"Dorm level 2 · 5 beds"A level bought took effectThe Dorm
"{name} is home", or "3 Umas are home"Swaps brought Umas homeThe Dorm

{name} is her name, from master data.

The roster's screens

  • The Dorm (the Dorm on the map):
    • its level and "3 of 5 beds"; with a bed free and someone in town, "A bed is free: invite someone from town on her profile."; after buying, "Level 3 tomorrow · 7 beds";
    • the next level, "Level 3 · 7 beds", with "· Farm Rank 2" while it's locked, and Upgrade · 3,000g (disabled until the Farm Rank is reached and the gold is there; "Need 500g more" when only gold is short); at the top, "The Dorm is at its top level.";
    • Levels: the eight, each "Built", "Tomorrow", or its cost and Farm Rank;
    • Tomorrow: each swap as two faces with "… arrives tomorrow" and Call off;
    • On the farm · n: each Uma with her best rank and "Farmhand", "Idle" (not working the farm) or "Leaves tomorrow";
    • Swap (or "Swap · nobody in town"), enabled when someone home can go and someone in town can come: a picker over the Umas home ("Who goes to town?", one leaving greyed out with why), then over those in town ("Who comes home for {name}?");
    • In town · n: each Uma with "In town" or "Arrives tomorrow", or "Nobody you’ve met lives in town." Every row opens her profile.
  • The Gate (its quick look on the map): "Everyone lives in town.", with the Town (see meeting her).
  • The roster is the People page's Work view (/hearts?view=work; /roster lands there): everyone on the roster, on the farm or in town, as an album of cards in three columns, under the line "3 on the farm · 2 in town · by joined".
    • Her face carries her favourite line's mark top left (a Carrot farming, a Pine Log foraging, a Carp fishing, Copper Ore mining, on a light disc), her best talent's grade bottom right on a coloured chip (S gold, A coral, B blue, C green, D and below grey), and her level in words top right ("F7", "R4", "S2").
    • Her card says her best rank ("Hand · Farming", or "Novice" before her first points) and her best talent ("Fishing A"). Sorted by an activity, it says her rank and talent in it ("Hand · C") and the activity, with her points when sorted by them ("Fishing · 52 pts"). An Uma in town is dimmed, and her second line says "In town".
    • Search by name.
    • Filters: Where (On the farm, In town); Favourite line (Farming, Mining, Fishing, Foraging); her talent in each activity (C+, B+, A+, S); her rank in each activity (Novice … Legend).
    • Sorts: Joined (the default, oldest first), Talent and Rank points (each in a chosen activity, the sort sheet's "In" chips, Farming until one is chosen; highest first), and Name.
    • A tap on her card opens her profile on its Work side: "Favourite · Fishing (×1.2 rank points)", "Joined Spring 1", and "On the farm" or "In town · the Dorm"; then her nine activities, each with its talent chip, her favourite mark on her favourite, "Hand · 52 / 160" (her points alone at Legend), and a line below: "Next: Catch 10 fish at the River · 3/10", "Points full · …", or "Ranks up tonight · …". A challenge whose target is 1 shows without a count.

The Hearts view, her profile's Hearts side, Chat and Gift are the romance's: see the People page and her profile.

The town

The cast lives in town from day 1: the owner's Umas, the story's cast (sisters included), in her default outfit or the pack's outfit for her. Nobody else from master data is in the game. The town is a map you walk around: going in takes one of the day's actions, and walking between its places is free. Each place lists who's there now. Nobody is placed at random: every Uma keeps a fixed schedule, the story's (her home, her part-time job, the places she likes, and when), else the game's default (her favourite spot every third day of each season), and her profile and the calendar show it. The town's people, fourteen Umas in the town's roles, are at their posts. Tap an Uma for her menu: you meet her by introducing yourself (see meeting her).

 the farm map ──► the Town: Go into town · 1 action (the town and its shop open for the day)
                    the map: its places (the six spots, the shops and offices, the story's workplaces and homes)
                      tap a place (free) ──► who's here now, with faces · its keeper at her post, and her post's menu
                        tap an Uma ──► her menu: Introduce yourself · 1 │ Talk · 1 │ Give a gift · 1 │ Ask her out · 2
                                                 │ Invite her to work · 1 │ Her event · 1, when it's Ready
                    People: everyone, met or not, where she is today · find by name · met first or by name

The map

The town's places, in four groups on the map:

  • You arrive at the plaza (/town?at=plaza), marked You're here. A tap on another place goes there (?at=<id>): free, and Back still returns to the farm.
  • Each place on the map shows its name, how many are there now on its badge, and their first four faces.
  • A crowded spot: a spot holds everyone the schedules put there, with no cap (with a cast of a hundred on the default schedule, about a third are out each day, five or six a spot, more where many share a favourite). Its tile keeps its count on the badge and its first four faces, then "+n"; where you are lists everyone.
  • Where you are, beside the map: its name and line, Here now · n with each Uma's face and name in a grid that scrolls (a ♥ on one you've met, a ✓ on one you've talked with today), or "No one's here now."; then its keeper at her post and her post's menu.
  • The top bar shows the actions left, as everywhere.

Who's where

Every Uma keeps a fixed schedule, and nothing is drawn. One pure function, scheduleOf(pack, id) (town/town.ts), gives her days and places; whereOn(pack, id, day) reads a day of it. The town, her profile ("Where to find her") and the calendar's days ("In town") all read it, so what her profile says is where you'll find her.

The rule
Her storyHer routine’s first stop that holds that day (its days of the season and its seasons), else her job on its days, else her home: she’s there. On a day none holds, she’s at home, off the map
The default scheduleAn Uma the story hasn’t scheduled (no routine, job or home on the map): at her favourite spot on every third day of each season, by her place in the cast (the 1st, 4th, 7th… on 1, 4, 7, 10; the 2nd, 5th, 8th… on 2, 5, 8, 11; the 3rd, 6th, 9th… on 3, 6, 9, 12); at home, off the map, the other days
Her favourite spotHer cast entry’s spot; else the 6 in turn by her place in the cast, 3 Umas each (the 1st to 3rd at plaza, the 4th to 6th at market…, the 19th starting again)
A spotHolds everyone the schedules put there, with no cap; nobody is placed at random
On the farmAn Uma living on the farm (the Dorm, a spouse’s house) is at the farm, never in town; so is a farmhand by day, after her part-time job
The town’s peopleEach at her post: her role’s shop or office, else the spot she keeps
Her lineOne a day (draw(uid, day, 'town-line:<id>', 0)) of her bible’s townLines, else the game’s 6: “Oh, hello! Out for a walk too?” “Lovely day for it, isn’t it?” “You’re from the farm up the road, aren’t you?” “I come here when I need to think.” “Good to see you, {farmer}.” “Busy day? Mine’s only just starting.”
  • Her story's schedule is hers when it's written (her life in town): her routine's first stop whose days of the season and seasons hold, else her job on its days, else her home. A location the map doesn't hold is passed over; with none left, the story hasn't scheduled her. On a day none of it holds, she's at home, off the map.
  • The default schedule plays until hers is written: her favourite spot (her cast spot, else the six in turn by her place in the cast) on every third day of each season, starting on day 1, 2 or 3 by her place in the cast, so a third of the cast is out each day and the next Uma in the cast is out the day after. The same four days every season.
  • A spot holds everyone the schedules put there; the map shows a crowded one by its count and "+n" (the map).
  • The posts: each of the town's people is at one post: the spot town.json has her keep, else her role's shop or office, else the spot the game has her keep. A place whose keeper is posted elsewhere shows none (with no town.json, the mayor is at her office and the shopkeeper at the store, so the plaza and the market show no keeper).
  • On the farm: an Uma who lives on the farm (the Dorm, or a spouse's house) isn't in town; neither is a farmhand by day (farmhands). Nobody else moves when one moves in. One you've met who lives in town keeps her days.

Her menu

Tap an Uma (?uma=<id>) for her menu, on the dialogue engine: her bust on the left (her portrait in the day's outfit, her face changing as she answers; else her master-data bust), and on the right her name, how she stands with you (her stage in the Uma loop with her ladder's level: "Not met yet", "Acquaintance · F1", "Friend · F3", "Farmhand · F5", "Sweetheart · R4", "Engaged", "Married · S2"), where she is (the place, and her job's words when she's at work), her line in the engine's line box, and her menu. Each item says its cost; one that isn't open is greyed with why. Back returns to the map.

ItemCostWhen
Introduce yourself1 actionYou haven’t met her: in town today, where she is now. She’s met, and her meeting scene plays
Talk1 actionMet: once a day each, +15. Her line for the day plays on the dialogue engine
Give a gift1 actionMet: once a day each, one good from the Warehouse at the stars you choose; her reaction plays on the dialogue engine
Ask her out2 actionsHer evening, its play and her scene: a hangout with a friend (Friendship 3 and up), a date with a sweetheart (Romance 1 and up) or a spouse. One evening a day across everyone
Invite her to work1 actionA friend (Friendship 3 and up), once: she’s a farmhand from today, on the farm by day
Her event1 actionMet, when her next event is Ready; a special day’s event actions pay first
  • Her line: before you talk today, her line for the day in town (her bible's townLines, else the game's six); after a talk, her talk for the day (her chats by stage, else her temperament's lines), her face happy; after a gift, her reaction ("“I love it!”", "“Oh, I like this.”", "“Thank you.”", or the disliked line), her face by her taste. {farmer} is your name.
  • Introduce yourself meets her: she joins the roster at Friendship 0 and keeps living in town, and her meeting scene plays over the town at once (her meeting scene).
  • Talk and Give a gift are the romance's acts: +15, and her taste's points (see her route), floating as "+15 ♥" by her name. They're the same acts as Chat and Gift on her profile, each an action there too, once a day each wherever you do them, and only where you meet her (below).
  • Ask her out · 2 opens her evening (?evening=<id>) while she's in front of you, it's open to her (a hangout with a friend from Friendship 3, a date with a sweetheart from Romance 1 or a spouse) and the day has its two actions: one evening a day across everyone, the same kinds and scenes as on the farm (the evening). Otherwise it says why: "From Friendship 3", "Spent this evening ✓", "Tonight's evening is spent", "That takes 2 actions.".
  • Invite her to work · 1, once she's a friend (Friendship 3): she's a farmhand from today, keeps her life in town, and is on the farm by day from now on. Her scene plays over the town as soon as the action is spent (the invitation to work); then her menu says "Farmhand ✓" and her line ("The farm? I'd love to. Count me in."), and "Works the farm ✓". Set her tasks on the work board in the Book (farmhands).
  • Her event shows once it's Ready, wherever she lives, and opens the event player over the town; it takes an action (a special day's event actions first), and with none left it doesn't start.
  • Not out now: "At home today. Out on Spring 7, at the Library." (her next day out, from her schedule; with her profile once met); one on the farm: "She lives on the farm. Her profile ›", or for a farmhand "On the farm by day, after her job. Her profile ›" (her menu still works there: she's in front of you).
  • One of the town's people (her face at her post, or on the People list) shows her role, what she does and her ties with the cast, each with its line, and her post's menu: the general store's Buy seeds › opens the shop; the mayor's office's Order a build › and Borrow or repay › both open the office's sheet, its orders and its loans side by side (loans); the rancher's Buy an animal waits for the ranch. Talking with her counts nothing and costs nothing.
  • A Town note an introduction, a talk or a gift unlocks says "Town note · title ›" there, a tap to it in the Diary: an introduction or a talk at a spot visits it (Town notes).

Where you meet her (the talk-and-gift rule, and the evening's): you talk with her, give her a gift, ask her out and invite her to work only where she's in front of you: on the farm when she lives there (the Dorm, or a spouse's house) or works it by day (a farmhand), else in town where she is today, once you've gone into town (whereYouMeet). Her menu in town is always where she is; her profile follows the same rule (her profile). Her Ready event plays from anywhere (her menu, her profile, the morning's Ready line, the calendar). Why: going into town takes one of the day's actions and the map shows who's where, so the town is where the day's talks happen, her schedule says which days, and an Uma on the farm is always in front of you; an event is her route's milestone, offered by the morning, the calendar and the People page, so a day she's at home never strands it.

The town's screen

  • The Town on the farm map opens /town, loaded on demand: Map and People in tabs (?view=people), and Shop. Its title is the town's name when the world gives one, else "Town". Before you go in today, Go into town · 1 action.
  • People: everyone in town and the town's people, each with her face and name and where she is today ("At the Café today", "At the Bakery, at work today", "At home · out Spring 7" with her next day out, "On the farm", "On the farm by day" for a farmhand who lives in town, "At the Café, most days" for a keeper, "In town"), and Met for anyone you've met. Find by name (any case, accents aside) and a sort: Met first (met, then talked most, then by name) or Name. A tap goes to her: to her place and her menu when she's out, else her menu ("At home today").
  • Lean: faces load as they scroll into view.

The save

The save's town holds, per character (a character id, or a sister's s<outfitId>), you and her by day: the days you've talked (talks), the last day you talked (last) and the last day you gave her a gift (gift), each 0 before any. Met or not is the roster (roster[id].joined the day you met her). The gift day grows v5 in place: an older v5's record reads it as 0, and nothing moves updatedAt.

Farmhands

A friend you invite to work is a farmhand (the invitation to work). She keeps her life in town, but she's on the farm by day: her part-time job comes first, then she's at the farm, in front of you every day from the invitation on, for talks, gifts and her evening (she isn't on the town's map, and her schedule's days in town stop mattering). She gives the farm a few actions a day on the tasks you set her on the work board, in the Book. She works them overnight, before the crops grow, so the morning says what she did. Any Uma can do any task, and each task has an aptitude: a level for her that starts from her talents and rises with every action she spends on it, bringing more goods, better stars and new tricks. As the farmhands take the farm's work over, your own ten actions turn to the valley and its people. The rules are farm/farmhands.ts.

 your day (10 actions)                 the night, step 2, first: each farmhand in the order you invited her
   invite her to work · 1 (a friend)     her part-time job (the story's, else a job at her favourite spot)
   the Book: the work board (free)       then her actions today: 3, +1 settled in, +2 a spouse, +1 well fed this morning,
     her tasks, in your order              all at the farm's pace (half, from the second short night of upkeep)
     harvest · water · plant ·           the field's tasks, the loads and the animals' care first, in your order, each
     clear and till   (a plot's rows)      until it's done; then her gathering and digging share what's left, a turn each
     load (a station)                    every action: a point of practice in its aptitude; her goods into the Warehouse
     forage · chop (an area)             her place today (where she spent the most) pays rank points, bonds, her line
     fish (a spot)                     then the crops grow: her watering counts tonight
     care for animals (the Coop or the Barn) · work a face (the Mine or the Quarry) · cook (the farmhouse kitchen)
 the morning: Work done · "Name · 4 of 4 actions · watered 6 tiles · caught 1 fish · +1 good"; Ranks · "Name · Watering 3"

Her day

Her actions
A farmhand’s day, after her part-time job (she’s on the farm by day; she works her actions overnight)3
Settled in: from 12 days after you invited her (a season)+1
Once she’s your spouse+2
Well fed this morning: a ★2+ meal last night, a household meal the day before, a wedding’s reception, a spouse’s breakfast+1
Half pace: from the second short night of upkeep in a row, until it’s paidHalf of them all, rounded down, never below 1
  • Her part-time job first: the story's job for her (her life in town), its words or where it is ("Waits tables", "Works at the Bakery"); else the game's default, a job at her favourite spot ("Helps out at the Café": her cast spot, else her default schedule's). It costs the farm nothing, and the board says it.
  • Then the farm, by day: after her job she's at the farm, so she's in front of you every day (where you meet her): Chat, Gift and her evening are on her profile and her menu without going into town. The town's map and People list say "On the farm by day"; her profile's Where to find her says "Today · On the farm" and "Every day · On the farm by day, after her job: Helps out at the Café". Her work is the night's, as before: the overnight work below is unchanged. At night she goes home to town (she takes no Dorm bed, and the morning's invitations stay those of the Umas home).
  • Settled in is a season after you invited her (roster[id].farmhand, the day you did). Married: once she's your spouse.
  • Well fed this morning: a household meal you cook today keeps everyone well fed today and tomorrow, and a ★2+ meal she eats from the pantry tonight through tomorrow (well fed, food); the night after notes the farmhands still well fed as the morning comes (today.fed), and each works one more action that day. So a meal gives one more action the next day, once: two meals the same day still give one, and a meal every day gives one every day. A wedding's reception and a spouse's breakfast, which feed the farm from the morning, give it that day.
  • Half pace reads the day as it was left: the night that counts the second short night still works at full pace, and the next day's is half (upkeep).
  • She costs 10g a night of upkeep, once, wherever she lives (in town, the Dorm or her house).
  • The farmer's ten are never touched. A task on her board is hers: you don't spend your actions on it.

The work board

The Book's Work board has a card for each farmhand, in the order they work: her face and name (a tap opens her profile's Work side), her job and the day she started, her actions today with what adds to them ("+1 settled in", "+1 well fed", "half pace" in red), Tonight, what she'll do as the farm stands now ("3 of 3 actions · watered 3 tiles"), and her tasks in your order, each with her aptitude's level ("Water · rows 1 and 2 · Watering 2"), ↑ to move it up and ✕ to remove it. Add a task (up to six) opens a side panel: what, then where (for the field's tasks, the plot (Plot 1, Plot 2 greyed with "Opens at Farm Rank 3" until it's cleared), then that plot's rows, each with what it holds for that task now, or the whole plot; the seed to plant, or any in season; a built station; the Coop or the Barn, built; the Mine or the Quarry, built; an area or a spot the farm reaches, the rest greyed with why; Cook has nowhere to choose: the farmhouse kitchen), and Add · Water · rows 1 and 2. A field task names its plot and works that plot's tiles only: once the farm has two plots, the board says which ("Water · plot 2, rows 1 and 2", "Water · all of plot 1"). The field's screen shows the same on each row's tab (the field's screen). Setting tasks is free and takes effect tonight; they repeat each day until you change them. With no farmhands: "No farmhands yet. Once she's a friend (Friendship 3), invite her to work: her menu in town."

TaskOverAn actionIt stopsAptitudeReads

ForageAn area the farm reachesA haul of 2 finds (3 from Foraging 5)Never: her gathering shares what’s left, a turn eachForagingForaging
ChopAn area the farm reaches2 logs (3 from Chopping 5)Never: her gathering shares what’s left, a turn eachChoppingForaging
FishA spot the farm reachesA cast: one fish (two casts from Fishing 6)Never: her gathering shares what’s left, a turn eachFishingFishing
Care for animalsThe Coop’s or the Barn’s animalsA hungry animal fed from the silo (two from Animal care 3), else grass cut on a weeds tile for it; then the building’s goods collected, +0.1 quality a level above 1; then an animal cared for (two from Animal care 5), +1 fondness a level above 1When every animal there is fed, collected and cared forAnimal careRanch
Work a faceThe Mine’s or the Quarry’s deepest floorA face on the deepest floor: 2 finds by her yield (3 from Mining 5), a face more toward its stairs; the farmer takes themNever: her digging shares what’s left, a turn eachMiningMining
CookThe farmhouse kitchen (the Kitchen’s, once it stands)A batch from 2 goods of food by the plain rule: 4 meals at Cooking 1, up to 12 at Cooking 10, into the pantryOnce the pantry holds tonight’s meals for everyone, or the food runs outCookingCooking
  • Her night, in order: the field's tasks, the loads, the animals' care and the cooking first, in your order, each until it's done for the day or her actions run out; then her gathering and digging tasks share what's left, a turn each. With nothing left to do she idles, and the morning says "Nothing to do tonight" (or "No tasks: set her tasks", tagged No tasks, with an empty board).
  • Two farmhands on the same tiles don't double up: the second finds them watered.
  • Cook: a batch an action from two goods of food, until the pantry holds tonight's meals; then her other tasks (food). The morning says "cooked 10 meals". The farm eats right after the farmhands work, so her meals feed everyone tonight.
  • Her reach is the farm's: the areas and spots the Farm Rank opens for the farmer (the sea's once their place stands), whatever her rank; at a dig, the deepest floor the farm has reached (her faces find its stairs; the farmer takes them).
  • The animals' care: in one task she feeds the building's hungry animals from the silo (cutting grass on a weeds tile when it's empty), collects its goods at her aptitude's quality, then cares for each, +8 fondness and +1 a level of Animal care above 1; a night of it counts toward her Ranch challenge, and an animal's hearts after her care toward its hearts challenges. The morning says "tended animals 4 times · +2 goods".
  • A face: her finds are the face's two × her mining rank's yield × her talent's × the Pick's or Hammer's × well fed's × her aptitude's, the fraction drawn, at her stars (her rank, talent, the tool, well fed, her keepsake, her aptitude); each face counts toward her Mining challenges by its finds and its floor. The morning says "worked 3 faces · +7 goods".
  • Her work's terms: a harvest draws its stars from the crop's level and the Hoe, her farming rank and talent, her care (+0.25), well fed (+0.25), her keepsake at her line's place and her aptitude; a haul's from her rank, talent, the tool, well fed, her keepsake and her aptitude. A haul's count is the task's × her rank's yield × her talent's × the tool's × well fed's (×1.25) × her aptitude's, the fraction drawn. Her seeds back are her rank's (what a rank gives). A load comes out at the input's stars, × her aptitude's yield.
  • What it counts: each action is a point of practice in its aptitude. Her harvests, hauls and catches count toward her challenges (the challenges), and the farm's lifetime counts, records and the Diary's depth milestones count her work as the farmer's. Her place today (where she spent the most actions, the first on a tie) pays her a day's rank points (rank points), bonds with the farmhands who worked it too, her line's +10 and a mentor's ×1.10; her goods are side by side's and the perks' to copy, and a ★3+ good earns her praise.
  • Every draw is seeded by the account, the day and the farm's counts, so a night replays the same on every device.

Aptitude

An aptitude is a level from 1 to 10 for each task, for each Uma, read from three parts and never stored as a level: - her head start, by her talent in the task's activity (her stats: talents); - her rank points in that activity: a point for every 20 (rank points); - her practice: a point for each action she spends on the task (roster[id].practice).

The points each level starts at, and what it gives (quality on her goods' stars, yield on their count):

Yield×1.00×1.05×1.10×1.15×1.20×1.25×1.30×1.35×1.40×1.45

Her head start by her talent:

  • A row an action is the design's "Water a row": at Watering 6 one action waters every dry tile of hers in the first row that has one.
  • Rising over days: three actions a day on one task take a D talent's Watering from 2 to 3 in about a week, and to 6 (the row) in about a year (the simulated year's field hand reaches it in Winter); the morning says each level reached ("Name · Watering 3 · Two tiles an action", tagged Aptitude).
  • Her profile's Work side lists her fifteen aptitudes with their level, a bar to the next and the trick it opened (or the next to come); before she's a farmhand it says "Aptitudes · where she'd start".

The save

Each farmhand's board is roster[id].tasks (in your order, at most six: the field's over a plot's tiles, 0–39, its plot plot (its index) written from plot 2 on, so a task from before plots, with none, is plot 1's; planting one crop or any; a station's load; an area's forage or chop; a spot's catch; the Coop's or the Barn's care; a dig's face; Cook; a task on a plot the farm hasn't cleared is refused), and each Uma's practice is roster[id].practice (actions spent, by aptitude). The farmhands well fed this morning are today.fed. They grow v5 in place: a save from before them reads as no tasks, no practice and nobody well fed, and nothing moves updatedAt for it. A farmhand's load may be bigger than the farmer's, so a station's load holds 1 to 20.

Talents and ranks

Every Uma is a specialist. Her talent in each activity, G to S, is read from her own stats by one rule, the same for everyone; it's why one Uma suits the Fields and another the Pond. Her rank in each activity, Novice to Legend, is earned by working and meeting a challenge; ranks are how the farm goes deeper and better.

The farmhands work. An Uma earns rank points and counts toward her challenges by working the farm as a farmhand (farmhands): one day's points a night in her place's activity, and her harvests, hauls and catches toward her challenges. One who doesn't work stays where she is. The farmer's own work counts toward no Uma's ranks, and his reach (and a farmhand's) is the Farm Rank's. Her talent also sets where each of her aptitudes starts, and her rank points add to them.

Talents

For each activity, her talent score is her 5★ base stat × (100 + her growth rate for that stat, in percent) ÷ 100, read from master data's outfit and kept to a millionth. Expeditions read the mean of her three best stat scores. Which stat each activity reads, and the temperament each top stat gives:

StatActivitiesTemperament (her top stat)
SpeedForaging, ServingBreezy
StaminaFarmingSteady
PowerMining, ForgingBold
GutsRanchGritty
WitFishing, CookingClever
All five: the mean of her three best scoresExpeditions—

The cut-offs are the same for everyone (150 is S; 149.9 is A):

GradeSABCDEFG
A stat’s score≥ 150≥ 138≥ 126≥ 114≥ 104≥ 96≥ 88< 88
Expeditions (mean of best three)≥ 136≥ 132≥ 129≥ 126≥ 124≥ 122≥ 120< 120
  • Her best talent (the chip on her face) is her highest grade among the eight one-stat activities, then the higher score, then the activities' order (farming, the ranch, mining, fishing, foraging, cooking, serving, forging). Expeditions, on their own scale, don't count.
  • Talents aren't saved. A fixed release always gives the same talents; the app computes the whole table once per release, and the night, the harvests and the screens all read it. An outfit a later release drops reads D everywhere.
  • On screen, until master data loads, everyone reads D.

Temperament

Her temperament is her top stat score's: Speed breezy, Stamina steady, Power bold, Guts gritty, Wit clever. A tie goes Speed, Stamina, Power, Guts, Wit. Her fallback chat lines (before her authored ones) read it.

What a talent gives

Each grade is a step, G 0 to S 7, and each step lifts her work:

TalentGFEDCBAS
Step01234567
Yield (gathering, stations)×0.85×0.90×0.95×1.00×1.05×1.10×1.15×1.20
Quality points−0.75−0.5−0.250+0.25+0.5+0.75+1
Rank points×0.8×0.85×0.9×1×1.1×1.2×1.3×1.5
Tended growth—————+0.05+0.1+0.15
Affection a day (ranch)+0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7
Expedition power+0+2+4+6+8+10+12+14
  • Yield multiplies what her gathering and station work brings.
  • Quality points add to the star draw of what she makes or grows.
  • Rank points multiply every pay of rank points.
  • Tended growth was a block's growth a night under her care (B and up only); the field's tiles grow a stage a watered night, so nothing reads it now.
  • Affection a day (the ranch) and expedition power are defined, but neither is read in play: a farmhand's care reads her Animal care aptitude (which her talent starts), and expeditions aren't open.

Rank points

Six ranks in each of the nine activities: Novice, Hand, Skilled, Expert, Master, Legend. A day of work pays rank points in its activity:

10 points × her talent's rank-point factor × 1.2 in her favourite line × 1.25 well fed × the hearts' factor

Points are kept to a millionth and shown whole. Every pay of rank points takes the same factors; a workshop play pays its assistant 10 and an expedition 10 a day (+10 on a Success, +20 on a Great), but no workshop or expedition is open in play. The ranch, the workshops and expeditions are nobody's favourite, so ×1.2 never applies there.

  • Well fed: an Uma with a meal's days left: a wedding's reception or a spouse's breakfast feeds the farm. The days count down each night.
  • The hearts' factor reads her friendship level:
×1×1.05×1.10
F0–F4F5–F9F10 on: her confession, romance and spouse

Ranks come at 40, 160, 400, 900 and 2,000 points. Days of work to reach each, by talent (in her favourite line in brackets), with every challenge met as it comes:

RankPointsGDCAS
Novice0—————
Hand405 (5)4 (4)4 (4)4 (3)3 (3)
Skilled16020 (17)16 (14)15 (13)13 (11)11 (9)
Expert40050 (42)40 (34)37 (31)31 (26)27 (23)
Master900113 (94)90 (75)82 (69)70 (58)60 (50)
Legend2,000250 (209)200 (167)182 (152)154 (129)134 (112)

Points go on past a threshold while the challenge waits; they also keep counting at Legend.

  • A mentor: an Uma at Friendship 9 working her line's place mentors the others who worked there that day: they earn ×1.10 rank points, she doesn't, one mentor a place. A day at her line also pays into her route. The romance part covers both.

A farmhand's day pays one activity: her place today's, where she spent the most actions (the first she worked on a tie):

  • One day pays one activity.
  • An Uma living in town earns nothing.
  • A farmhand's day at the Mine, the Quarry, the Smelter or the Cutter pays mining, and at the Coop or the Barn the ranch; cooking, serving, forging and expeditions wait for their places.

The challenges

A rank comes when its points are reached and its challenge is met. Each depth challenge is "work the deepest place your rank allows", so ranking up is how the farm goes deeper. The challenge toward each rank, by activity:

ActivityHandSkilledExpertMasterLegendCounted by
FarmingHarvest 30 crops she tended40 crops of ★3+ she tendedTend a crop of level 4+ to harvest30 crops of ★51st at a Flower Show with a crop she tendedAt harvest, for the tender; a festival win (festivals aren’t built yet)
RanchCare for animals 10 daysAn animal in her care reaches 4 heartsAn animal in her care reaches 7 heartsAn animal in her care reaches 10 hearts1st at an Animal Show with an animal in her careA farmhand’s night at the Coop or the Barn; a farmhand’s care for an animal; a festival win (festivals aren’t built yet)
MiningDig 40 ores or stonesDig at floor 10Dig at floor 15Dig at floor 20Dig at floor 25A farmhand’s faces at the Mine or the Quarry
FishingCatch 30 fishCatch 10 fish at the RiverCatch 10 fish at the LakeCatch 10 fish at the Falls1st at a Fishing Derby, on the teamThe night’s catches (night step 4); a festival win (festivals aren’t built yet)
ForagingGather 50 itemsGather 20 items in the HillsGather 20 items in the Old ForestGather 20 items on the MountainGather 20 items in the Misty ValeThe night’s hauls (night step 4)
CookingAssist 5 Kitchen playsAssist a Fine playMake 10 dishes of ★4+Assist a Masterwork1st at a Cooking Contest as the cookThe Kitchen (not built yet); a festival win (festivals aren’t built yet)
ServingAssist 5 shiftsAssist a Fine shiftServe 100 guestsAssist a Masterwork shiftServe 1,000 guestsThe Restaurant (not built yet)
ForgingAssist 5 Forge playsAssist a Fine mergeForge a tool upgradeAssist a MasterworkForge a Mythril toolThe Forge’s workshop (not built yet)
ExpeditionsReturn from 2 expeditionsA Success at level 10+A Success at level 20+A Great at level 20+A Success at the Sky RuinsExpeditions (not built yet)
  • One challenge at a time: her count is toward her next rank's challenge only, from her joining or her last rank-up in that activity. It stops at the target and resets to 0 at the rank-up.
  • What counts it: her own work, once the farmhands work. The farmer's harvests, hauls and catches count toward no Uma's challenge. Each challenge reads only its own kind of event; an Uma off the roster or at Legend counts nothing.
  • In play: farming's, foraging's, fishing's, the ranch's and mining's challenges are counted: the ranch's by a farmhand's night at the Coop or the Barn and the hearts of an animal she cares for, mining's by her faces' finds and floors. The others wait for places that aren't open. Farming's, fishing's and the ranch's Legend challenges are festival wins, and no festival is held, so those climbs stop at Master; foraging and mining can reach Legend.

Rank-ups at night

Night step 6:

  1. Points: each Uma who worked earns a day's points in its activity, with her factors as she worked (well fed is read before the night counts it down).
  2. Rank-ups: every Uma on the roster, in every activity, ranks up where her points have reached the next rank's and its challenge is met. One rank a night per activity; the challenge's count resets. A challenge met during the day waits for that night. A rank skipped by points still takes its challenges one at a time, a rank a night.
  3. Lines: the rank-ups first, then points full: an Uma whose points for her next rank filled tonight with its challenge still to meet. It's said once, the night they fill (or the night a rank-up leaves the next rank already full); her sheet keeps saying "Points full" until she ranks up.

A rank reached tonight lifts her work from tomorrow.

The morning's Ranks section lists the night's lines, each opening her sheet:

LineWords
A rank-up"{name} · Farming Hand", tagged Rank up
Points full"{name} · Fishing · Catch 30 fish · 12/30", tagged Points full: the challenge in its own words with her count; a one-off challenge has no count ("Dig at floor 10")

  • Yield multiplies her gathering and station work.
  • Tended growth was a block's growth a night under her care; nothing reads it now.
  • Quality adds to the star draw of what she grows or makes.
  • Deepest is the last area, spot and floor her rank lets her work.
  • Seeds back is her harvest's chance of a seed, before the Hoe.
  • Expedition power and workshop yield are defined for places that aren't open in play; affection a day isn't read (a farmhand's care reads her Animal care aptitude).

Her favourite line

Her favourite line is her story bible's, else her cast entry's calling (a quarry calling is mining, at the Quarry), read from the story pack: farming, foraging, fishing or mining. She earns ×1.2 rank points there. The mark on her face is her favourite line's.

  • She can work anywhere. Her favourite only speeds her ranks; nothing refuses her elsewhere.
  • The ranch, the workshops and expeditions are nobody's favourite.
  • What else her line does (her perks, her tastes in gifts) is the romance's: see her favourite line and tastes.

The farm's progress

The farm grows by what you earn and grow, and by your life with the Umas. There are no global chapters and no farm-wide story: the story lives in each Uma, in her own chapters. Four parts, all checked at night: - the Farm Rank, the farm's own ladder from 1 to 10, from income and resources only; - the Diary, which counts what you ship and pays milestones; - building: the Farm Rank offers each place past the start, and you build it with gold and the farm's goods; - the milestones with the Umas, which open the homestead and the town.

The Diary's projects gather them: what you can build now, what the next rank offers, and the milestones with the Umas.

 the day: plant, water, gather, ship ──► the Farm Rank offers a place ──► its Build card: gold + goods ──► Build
                                                                                                         │
 End the day ──► night step 8: ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                   1. the places paid for stand ("Built overnight · the Mill")
                   2. the Diary pays what's reached (milestones, rows)
                   3. the Farm Rank's points are recomputed; a new rank offers more
                   4. the ring bench, the night after the first Romance 8
                   ▼
 the morning: "Built overnight · the Mill", "Farm Rank 2 · The Coop to build (2,000g · 30 logs · 20 vegetables), …", "First Uma at Hand · +300g"

 your life with the Umas: the n-th wedding ──► House n · 3 married ──► the homestead's second row · 5 met ──► the board

The Farm Rank

The farm's own rank, 1 to 10. Its points are recomputed from the save each night at step 8, from income and resources only:

⌊gold shipped ÷ 100⌋ + 5 × Diary entries + 25 × milestones

Every term only grows, so the rank never falls. During the day the rank stays as last night left it: what you ship today counts tonight. Where the points come from:

SourcePointsRead from
Gold shipped at the bin1 per 100gfarm.counts['gold-shipped']
A Diary entry: a good shipped once5diary.shipped
A milestone25diary.milestones

Festivals and decorations add nothing in play: neither exists. The points each rank needs, tuned when Umas worked the farm so the ranks came at the pace they came at when each global chapter added 250 points (the farmer's pace alone is the simulated first season's, and two farmhands reach Farm Rank 3 by Summer's end: see the pacing):

Farm Rank12345678910
Points01502509502,1503,7506,2509,75014,75021,750

What each rank opens is read from the rules that gate on it (the places it offers to build, the Fields' blocks, the Dorm's levels, the shop's seed tiers), so the sheet and the morning can't drift from the game:

Two ranks in one night are two lines, lowest first.

The Diary

The Diary counts what the farm has shipped and pays for it.

  • Entries: each good shipped at least once is an entry, worth 5 Farm Rank points. Seeds never ship, so they're never entries.
  • Collection rows: a family's ten raw goods, or its ten refined ones, each shipped at least once, completes a row; the ten rings are a row of their own, the 27th. A row pays 500g once. It isn't a milestone and adds no points of its own (its goods are already entries). Each ring shipped is an entry.
  • Milestones each pay their gold once and are worth 25 Farm Rank points.
  • When they pay: milestones and rows are checked at night step 8 and paid that night, their gold with the bin's, milestones first in their list order, then rows. Each is listed in the morning's New today ("10,000g shipped · +300g", "Woods: every grade shipped · +500g"), tagged Milestone, opening the Diary.
  • Umas on the roster counts everyone you've met, on the farm or in town, day 1's visitor included, so a fifth Uma pays 300g.
  • Depth counts each forest area and fishing spot past the first. It pays the first night a haul comes home from there; the Woods and the Pond are open on day 1 and pay none.
  • Ranks pay for the first Uma to reach each rank in any activity.

Building

Every place past the start is offered at its Farm Rank and built with gold and the farm's own goods:

  1. Offered: the Farm Rank reaches its rank (the Boat once the Dock stands too).
  2. Paid: its Build card (the place's quick look, or a station's page), or the mayor's office in town, with your gold or a loan for what's short (loans), takes its gold and goods now. Each goods line takes any grade and any stars of its family, the cheapest first (★1 price × the star's factor, then fewer stars, then the catalogue's order). The place waits in farm.builds.
  3. Built: the next morning, night step 8 sets buildings[id] to 1 before anything else, and New today says "Built overnight · the Mill", tagged Built, opening its quick look (the Boat's opens the Dock's).

A place whose game isn't built yet shows what it'll be: its Build card has its cost, "Not built yet", and a disabled Not built yet button. It can't be bought. The places, their ranks and their costs:

PlaceFarm RankGoldGoodsCan be bought
Mill11,000g20 logsYes
Coop22,000g30 logs, 20 vegetablesYes
Sawmill31,500g40 logsYes
Barn33,500g30 logs, 20 vegetablesYes
Smokehouse32,000g30 logs, 20 fishYes
Mine410,000g60 planksYes
Smelter48,000g40 planks, 40 logsYes
Kitchen515,000g50 planks, 30 vegetablesNot built yet
Quarry512,000g50 planksYes
Cutter510,000g40 planks, 40 logsYes
Dock620,000g80 planksYes
Boat630,000g60 planks, 30 filletsYes, after the Dock
Restaurant740,000g80 planks, 40 fillets, 30 vegetablesNot built yet
Expedition post725,000g60 planks, 40 filletsNot built yet
Forge860,000g120 planks, 60 logsNot built yet
  • Refusals: built already or paid today ("Builds tonight."); not built yet ("Not built yet."); its Farm Rank ("Opens at Farm Rank 3."); the place before it ("After the Dock."); short ("Need 400g and 12 logs more.").
  • The Dock and the Boat open the sea: the Sea Pier needs the Dock, the Deep Sea the Boat (see the Fishery's spots). The Dock stands beside the Gate; the Boat moors there, built from the Dock's quick look. Both have place ids, so a story's built gate can name them.
  • What used a chapter now uses the place it meant: the ring bench takes ingots and cut gems once the Smelter and the Cutter stand; a perk or a working setup at her line's place says "Opens when the Kitchen is built" (or her place); the sea's spots need the Dock and the Boat.
  • Never stamped by the rule: paying is the player's change, stamped once by the app.

The farm's projects

The Diary's Projects card gathers what's next: - Loans, first while there are any, each a goal: what it built, "440g owed · 45g a night · clears in about 10 nights (Spring 14)"; a tap opens the Book (see loans). - Build now: every place offered at your rank and not standing, whose game is built: its name, its cost ("1,000g · 20 logs"), and what it still lacks ("Need 400g and 12 logs more"), "Builds tonight" once paid, or Build ›. A tap opens its quick look and Build card. With none: "Nothing to build at this rank." When a place lacks only gold, "Short of gold? The mayor's office lends toward a build. ›" opens the office in town. - Not built yet: the places offered at your rank whose games aren't built, in one line ("Not built yet: the Kitchen"). - At Farm Rank 3 · 250 points: what the next rank offers, each with its cost or "Not built yet". - With the Umas: each milestone, a check when it's done, what it waits for and how far along you are: "The ring bench · The first Romance 8 · Not yet", "House 1 · Your first wedding · 0 / 1 married", "The homestead's second row · 3 married · 1 / 3 married", "The Requests board · 5 Umas met · 3 / 5 met". The house shown is the next to open.

The pacing

A test (packages/domain/src/farm/season.test.ts) plays the first year on the game's own rules, the farmer working alone, by a plain bot, ten actions a day. Each morning it harvests what's ripe, waters what grows, plants the tilled soil it has seeds for, tills and clears a little more while fewer than five tiles grow, ships once there's a crate's worth (less the logs and fish its next build takes), goes into town for the season's quickest seeds when they run out (keeping tonight's bill back), and spends what's left fishing, foraging and chopping (chopping first while the next build waits for logs). It builds as it goes, never into a night it can't pay: the Mill on a loan from the mayor's office as soon as it holds the logs and the gold left covers tonight's bill, then the Farm Rank 3 stations. Nobody lives on the farm: an Uma there eats, and one with no work costs her upkeep and her meals for nothing. Every night the farm eats, then pays its bill: the loan's share, the takeout and the upkeep. The farmer eats takeout while his gold holds, and cooks a batch from his two cheapest goods of food when it won't cover a few nights' takeout. It checks: - every day spends exactly its ten actions, and every morning has ten again; - watered crops grow and dry ones don't, and something is harvested; - the season's last night withers what's left (only Corn and Cabbage carry over); - the bin pays: over 1,000g shipped in Spring, gold above the 500g start, and Farm Rank points earned; - the Mill stands early on a loan, and Spring runs a surplus over its takeout, upkeep and loan; - the bill is always paid: never a short night, never half pace; - every season earns more than its takeout, upkeep and loans take, and the year ends richer than it started; - the loan clears, and the farmer alone reaches Farm Rank 2; - takeout bites from the first night (the first morning says "Takeout · 30g") and comes to a sixth of what he ships; he cooks to bridge the Mill's tight night, and the year ends within a tenth of the same farm on takeout alone; - every morning's save parses as v5.

The run as it stands (SEASON=1 prints it, and the same farms on takeout alone beside them). Spring, day by day:

The year, season by season (the morning after each season's last night):

SeasonThe bin paidTakeoutUpkeepThe loan's sharesLeftShort nightsGold afterUpkeep a night, at its endFarm Rank
Spring1,140g270g105g160g605g0625g15g (the Mill)1

Winter2,955g360g180g—2,415g04,850g15g2
  • The loan builds the Mill early: 20 logs held by Spring 4, but the 10g a loan would leave him that day wouldn't pay the night's 30g of takeout, so he waits a day: on Spring 5, 380g of his own and a 700g loan (770g owed, 80g a night), and with his gold low he keeps an action for the stove and cooks a batch from the day's hauls: four meals, his two nights' dinners. The Mill stands on Spring 6; the nights' income clears the loan in Autumn.
  • Takeout bites, and never sinks him: 1,320g of takeout in the year (88 meals) against 9,005g shipped, a sixth, with never a short night. On takeout alone (no batch) the year ends on 4,655g; cooking for the tight nights, on 4,850g. Two goods are worth about the four meals of takeout they save, so alone he buys his food and cooks only to bridge a night (food).
  • Year 2 opens on 4,850g, 9,005g shipped, 47 tiles harvested and 165 Farm Rank points (Farm Rank 2). The farmer alone doesn't reach Farm Rank 3 (250) in a year, so the Sawmill and the Smokehouse wait for the farmhands.
  • Fishing and foraging still carry the farm: an action at the water or the woods pays about what a Carrot's whole run of six actions does.
With two farmhands

The same test plays the same year a second time with two farmhands, friends from town invited to work on Spring 2, who live in town and cost 10g of upkeep each a night from then on. One waters the whole field; the other forages and fishes at the deepest area and spot the farm reaches (the Hills and the River from Farm Rank 2). Both have a D talent everywhere. Two Umas live on the farm from Summer, three from Autumn and four from Winter (the Dorm's second level for the fourth), though none of them works it. The farmer keeps a field twice the size (ten tiles growing), plants and harvests it himself, waters only what she won't reach tonight (as the board's Tonight shows), gathers no more than the four actions a day he did alone, and spends the rest of his day with the people in town. The field hand is the Cook: her board is the whole field's watering, then Cook, so once the field's watered her actions left cook a batch each until the pantry holds tonight's meals; the farmer keeps back from the bin the goods her batches will take. The same farm on takeout alone runs beside it. It checks: - their upkeep and every night's bill are paid: never a short night, never half pace, on takeout alone too; four Umas home and the two farmhands on the upkeep; - they work every night from Spring 2, three actions or more between them, and each morning reports them; - every season earns more than its takeout, upkeep and loans take, and more than the farmer alone's; the year ships more and ends richer; - his own day is freed: his watering is less than half of what it was alone, more crops grow, and over two of his actions a day go to the people; - their aptitudes rise: the field hand reaches Watering 6 (a row an action) by the year's end, and the gatherer Foraging 4 and Fishing 4; - takeout bites: on takeout alone it's between a sixth and three tenths of what the farm ships, and never sinks it; - cooking pays for itself once the field yields: less takeout each season from Summer, under a third of the takeout farm's by Winter, twice as many meals from the pantry as bought in, the cook at Cooking 3, more than twelve well-fed nights in Winter and nothing spoiled; the year ends richer than on takeout, and Autumn and Winter each earn more after their takeout; - every morning's save parses as v5.

SeasonThe bin paidTakeoutUpkeepLeftShort nightsGold afterUpkeep a night, at its endFarm Rank
Spring3,885g840g265g2,780g02,100g35g (the Mill, two farmhands)1
Summer8,690g1,755g660g6,275g010,215g55g (and two Umas home)3
Autumn8,395g1,095g1,025g6,275g011,750g105g (the Dorm's level 2, the Sawmill, the Smokehouse)3
Winter15,525g750g1,380g13,395g024,605g115g (four home)3

The same farm on takeout alone pays 1,020g, 1,800g, 2,160g and 2,520g of takeout and ends the year on 17,280g.

  • They pay their way many times over: their 20g a night is part of the year's 3,330g upkeep, against 36,495g shipped (four times the farmer alone's 9,005g). The farmhands spent 371 actions in the year; the farmer watered 21 tiles (156 alone) and spent 103 actions with the people.
  • The farm grows faster: Farm Rank 3 by Summer's end, the Mill on Spring 9 and the Dorm's second level, the Sawmill and the Smokehouse in Autumn, all on his own gold, and 659 Farm Rank points by Year 2.
  • The cook pays once the field yields: in Spring and Summer the field takes all the field hand's actions, so the farm buys its food; as her Watering rises (two tiles an action, then a row) her actions left cook, the pantry feeds the farm (204 meals in the year, 118 in Winter alone), and her ★2 batches keep the farmhands well fed. The year ends 42% richer than on takeout.
  • Where it comes from: the gatherer works the Hills and the River once Farm Rank 2 opens them, where a haul is worth two or three of the Woods', and her foraging rank reaches Skilled (×1.3 yield, +1 quality) on her challenges.
The whole farm: the Coop and the Mine

The same test plays a third run, two years of the farm with its two farmhands (the field hand on Cook) that also builds the ranch and the Mine: the Coop as soon as the Farm Rank offers it and its logs and vegetables are held (from Summer, growing vegetables for it), four chickens there with fodder bought in town when the silo runs low (keeping 600g back), and a third friend invited to care for them the day it stands (feed, collect, care); then, in its second year, the Mine (its 60 planks sawn from his logs at the Sawmill, a load a day), a fourth friend at its face, and the stairs taken whenever she finds them. The Coop and the Mine add their 15g a night each and their farmhands' 10g each; the chickens cost their 500g and their feed; the cook feeds them all. It checks: - two years without a short night, never half pace; - every season earns more than its takeout, upkeep and loans take, and the two years end richer than one year with two farmhands; - the Coop stands in the first year and the Mine in the second, both counted in the upkeep, with their farmhands; - the eggs pay more than the Coop's nights cost (its upkeep, its farmhand's, the hens and their feed), and the ores more than the Mine's (its upkeep and its farmhand's); - each morning reports the farmhands at the Coop and the Mine and the Coop's night, nobody hungry, and the Mine's deepest floor reaches floor 10 or deeper; - every morning's save parses as v5.

SeasonThe bin paidTakeoutUpkeepLeftShort nightsGold afterUpkeep a night, at its endFarm Rank
Spring 13,885g840g265g2,780g02,100g35g1
Summer 14,820g1,755g660g2,405g05,865g55g3
Autumn 18,755g960g1,260g6,535g03,160g130g (the Coop and its farmhand, the Dorm's level 2, the Sawmill, the Smokehouse)3
Winter 120,830g675g1,680g18,475g020,695g140g3

Autumn 281,350g60g1,980g79,310g0166,155g165g5
