  • The Coop pays its way three times over: it is built on Autumn 1 of the first year (the vegetables it takes come with Summer's crops), and over its 71 nights its eggs sell for 27,375g against 6,975g of upkeep, its farmhand, the four hens and their fodder. Its farmhand reaches Animal care 9 and the ranch's Expert rank.
  • The Mine pays at once: it is built on Spring 11 of the second year; its farmhand's faces and the farmer's free stairs reach floor 15 (the Farm Rank's reach), and over its 37 nights its ores sell for 88,025g against 925g of its nights' costs.
  • The cook feeds the farm: from the first Winter the pantry covers nearly every meal (takeout 60–405g a season in the second year), the cook reaches Cooking 6, and her ★2 batches keep the four farmhands well fed nearly every night. That's most of why the second year ships 256,275g, nearly twice the 137,600g it shipped before food: a well-fed farmhand works one more action, with ×1.25 hauls and +0.25 quality.
  • The farm in all: the two years ship 294,565g and end on 263,780g, Farm Rank 5 (3,470 points); every build is paid from its own gold.
  • No retune: the ranch's and the Mine's numbers stand as built, and so do food's: takeout at 15g a meal, the farmer's batch of four from two goods, the cook's 4 to 12. F91 (work at scale) takes the farm's work and prices on from here.
A route to marriage

How long marriage takes, answered the same way: a test (packages/domain/src/romance/marriage.test.ts) plays the game's own rules with a plain bot, ten actions a day, courting one default-story Uma from meeting her to the wedding night and farming with what's left. MARRIAGE=1 prints the tables below.

  • The town is honest, and nothing is drawn. The cast there is a hundred invented characters, as the owner's is, each on the default schedule, so about a third are out at the six spots each day and the rest at home, as on the phone. She has no home, job or routine, so she keeps the default schedule: her favourite spot on four days a season (the first in the cast: days 1, 4, 7 and 10). A second way gives her cast entry a home in town (a town.json home on the map), so she's out every day.
  • She's met only by meeting her: introducing yourself in town, on a day she's out. Talks, gifts, Praise, her evening and the invitation to work need her in front of you: on the farm when she lives there or works it by day (a farmhand), else in town where she is today, where the bot asks her out. The bot never forces her into town: a day she's at home it farms instead, and those days are counted. Her events play from anywhere.
  • Each day, while she's in front of you (going into town for her when she's out there): a talk, a gift, Praise when she's earned it, and from Friendship 3 the invitation to work (a farmhand from then, on the farm by day, watering the field and foraging the woods overnight); her morning invitation said yes; her evening when it's open (2 actions); her event when Ready (the warmest reply, Yes at her confession); from Romance 8 a ring at the bench from three refined goods the Mill made (the bot keeps the Mill's goods from Romance 5); then the proposal and the wedding. Then the farm, as the first year's bot plays it: harvest, water what she won't, the Mill on a loan once its logs are held, the season's quickest seeds, a crate shipped, and the rest at the woods and the water.
  • Two profiles. Devoted: her loved gift whenever it's held, else a liked one; Great evenings in her favourite kind. Casual: a liked gift; Fine evenings, the first kind offered. A default-story Uma loves her line's good and likes nothing in particular, so her tastes for the run are a synthetic bible's: loved her line's default (a farming Uma: Carrot, the season's quickest seed in Spring), liked four goods of the woods. Her favourite kind is the walk, which both profiles take.
  • Two homes. She stays in town (a farmhand from Friendship 3, still living there), or the bot invites her to the farm (a free Dorm bed) the day she's met.
  • Seven farms each: the account seeds every draw (stars, hauls, her lines, her invitations), never where she is, so every farm meets her on day 1. Each cell is the median of the seven, with their range. A day is the farm's (48 to a year).

It checks that every run reaches the wedding night, that nothing dead-ends (her event only ever waits on her bar, the day after, a ring or one a day), that the farm is no slower than town and devoted no slower than casual, that a farmhand and an Uma on the farm are in front of you every day, that every farm meets her the same day, that a home in town brings her out every day and weds no later, and that living in town she weds inside the second year.

  • Living in town, the wedding comes in the first year or early in the second, no longer in years 4–7: devoted on the default schedule, day 50 (Spring 2, year 2), 49 days after meeting her, some 3–4 hours of play; casual, day 72 (Summer 12, year 2). With a home in town, devoted weds on day 36, the farm's own pace.
  • The default schedule brings her out a third of the days (36% before she's a farmhand: four days of each season's twelve), the same days every season and on every farm, so you can plan for them: her profile and the calendar show them. Those days are the whole of her wait before Friendship 3 (16 days out of reach, devoted).
  • The farmhand is the turn. Once she's invited to work (day 25, devoted), she's on the farm by day and in front of you every day (100%), so from then the town route runs like the farm's: the confession 11 days later (on the farm, 10), the wedding 25 days after she's a farmhand (on the farm, 25).
  • Her evening now plays in town. She has 26 evenings before the wedding, devoted, living in town as on the farm, where she used to have none: asked out where she is on her days, then every day once she's a farmhand.
  • Invited to the farm (free, the day she's met, with a bed free), she's in front of you every day and the wedding comes on day 36 (Autumn 12, year 1), 35 days after meeting her, devoted, or 47 casual, some 2–4 hours of play. A schedule changes nothing for an Uma on the farm (both columns alike).
  • A home in town (or any fuller schedule the story writes) is the story's lever for the first weeks: out every day, she reaches Friendship 3 on day 10, as on the farm, and the town route matches the farm's (devoted 35 days, casual 58).
  • Her loved gift is early now: a farming Uma loves Carrot, the first season's quickest seed, so devoted gives it on ten days before the wedding (in town; nine on the farm). Every default loved good can be had in Spring of year 1 (her favourite line and tastes).
  • The ring never holds her long: the bench opens the night after Romance 8, and the Mill's goods kept from Romance 5 make a ring there; the proposal waited on it 0–2 days at the median, 10 at most.
  • No retune: the owner decides the pace after reading it.

The night's goals check

Night step 8 runs, in order:

  1. The places paid for yesterday stand (raiseBuildings), each a line of news.
  2. The Dorm settles (see the roster).
  3. The Diary pays every milestone and row reached and not yet paid.
  4. The Farm Rank's points are recomputed; the ranks reached tonight are noted, lowest first.
  5. The ring bench opens the night after the first Romance 8.

The morning's New today then lists, in order: the season and festivals, each place built, each Farm Rank reached, each milestone and row paid, then the household's milestones and the Dorm's news.

The town's people

The town's people are Umas: fourteen roles, each played by a character from master data. The story's town.json says who plays each role, by her character id (its game is the role's id below); a role it leaves goes to one of master data's characters, from the last in its order, never one of the cast. Her name and bust are master data's, at run time, so no name is in git. They live in town, never on the farm, and keep its spots (see the town).

The bell’s keeperbell-keeperKeeps the chapel bell
  • In a scene, a say by one of them, by her character id or by her role's (ida-stone), shows her name and bust. With no one playing a role (no master data), the role speaks in words ("The smith") on a lettered disc.
  • The smith speaks at the ring bench. The others have no lines of the game's; the opening is the story's, and any of the town's people may speak in it.
  • A tie with one of them builds no bond: its line shows on the cast Uma's profile ("In town ›") and on the town person's sheet.

The scenes

Each scene plays once: played through or skipped, it's marked read and never plays again. Which scene plays now:

  • Nothing plays until the farmer is named.
  • The opening's lines, on day 1, once you're named: the pack's opening.json, any length, else the game's two (below). Reading them or skipping them opens the farm on Home: no card, no tutorial, and nobody at the Gate.
  • The ring bench's scene, the morning after the first Romance 8 event, once the summary is put away: after the opening, before the meeting scenes.
  • Meeting scenes, after those, in the order you met them: at once after you introduce yourself in town.

The opening's lines

The pack's opening.json is the opening, at any length (see the opening). Without one, the game's own two play, one to a tap (its old third line, someone at the Gate, is retired with the welcome week):

#KindLine
1NarrationMorning, {farmer}. The farm is yours now, weeds and all.
2NarrationOne block is tilled, and nine Carrot seeds wait in the Fields.

The town's smith first appears at the ring bench, by her master-data name. The romance part covers the bench; its scene's lines are:

The Ring Bench (scene:bench)

#WhoLine
1(narration)A cart creaks up from town and stops at the old Forge.
2The smith“I keep the smithy down in town. They told me this Forge was still standing.”
3(narration)She runs a hand along the cold hearth, then looks at you.
4The smith“The fire can wait for another year. But a bench by the door, that I can set up today.”
5The smith“Bring three refined goods of one grade, and you’ll make a ring of that grade. Planks, fillets, flour: whatever you have.”
6The smith“One ring a day, and no charge. Word in town is that someone on this farm has your heart.”
7(narration)The Forge’s door stays open. A small bench waits inside, by the light.

The Diary tab

The Diary tab (/diary) holds the world's pages, the farm's projects, the Farm Rank and the milestones.

  • The farm (first, when the world names the farm, the town or the region, or gives a history): the farm's name (else "The farm"), the town and the region under it, and the farm's history, a paragraph a line.
  • Town notes (when the world writes any): the notes you know, in the pack's order, each a tap to read its lines (?note=<id> opens one, as the morning's line does), "None yet." before the first, and "2 of 5 found".
  • Projects: what to build now, what's not built yet, the next rank's offers and the milestones with the Umas (see the farm's projects).
  • Farm Rank · 400 points › opens the rank's sheet.
  • Milestones: each milestone and row paid, in the order paid, with its gold ("10,000g shipped · +300g"), or "None yet."; then "12 goods shipped so far".
  • Memories (each Uma's) and Conversations (the pairs'): the romance part covers them.
  • Its help says how building, the milestones with the Umas, the Diary's milestones, Memories and Conversations work.

The Farm Rank's card and sheet

  • The card is the Book's second, after the gold: the rank and its points ("1 · 47 points"); a tap opens the sheet. The Diary's Farm Rank row opens it too.
  • The sheet opens over any page: "Farm Rank 2", the points, a bar to the next rank with "50 more for Farm Rank 3 (250)" (or "The top rank"), what the next rank opens (the places to build first), and the points so far by source: Shipping (1 per 100g), Diary entries (5 each), Milestones (25 each). Its help explains where points come from.

The scene player

  • A full-screen sheet over the farm and its bars, on the dialogue engine: the scene's title and Skip at the top; on the left the speaker's bust (her portrait in the day's season with the line's face, else her bust from master data; a role's disc; none for narration); on the right the speaker's name in the accent colour and the line (narration in italics), and "Tap to go on · 3/12". A tap on the line or the bust goes on.
  • It loads master data itself (names, busts and portraits), since a new farm's boot doesn't wait for it. Skip works without it, and skipping marks the scene read.
  • The opening plays its lines with the title line "Day 1" (the world's farm and town after it when it names them, "Day 1 · Thistle Farm, Fernwick"; a place once a place step names one: "Day 1 · Gate"), "Tap to go on · 1/2". Its last tap, or Skip on any line from the first, reads it and opens Home: two taps after the name for the game's own two.
  • The same player plays the meeting scenes and the ring bench's scene; heart events play in their own player. The romance part covers them.

Part 3 — Hearts: the cast and the romance

The romance is the game's. It plays on save v5 from the story pack, and with the default story wherever the pack is silent: anyone in town can be met by introducing yourself there, and People is the rail's third page (Farm · Town · People · Book · Diary), with the roster's album as its Work view. A save from before v5 starts over as a new farm (see Save versions).

The dialogue engine

One engine plays every scene: the opening, meeting her, the ring bench's scene, heart events (and Ask her, and a Memory replayed), the wedding night and moving in, pair conversations, the household's scenes and the finale, the calendar's occasion scenes (occasion scenes), and the Uma loop's: each evening's scene after its play (a hangout's, a date's, Dinner in town's and A night in's, handing in their moment: a picnic, a dinner, a night in, a festival night), the night's companion's morning greeting (pajamas) and a meal's line (the Uma loop). The plays have no lines. The story's steps are the same in every scene (say, narrate, thought, choice, play, …). At her big moments the scene opens onto her illustration, full screen (her illustrations), and each one seen is kept in her gallery (her gallery).

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ The Fields · Friendship 2                             Auto  Skip  Log │
│ ╭──────────────────────────╮                                         │
│ │ (the place's    ┌──────┐ │                                         │  her portrait in the scene's

  • Landscape: her bust on the left, the line or the choices on the right, both resting on the foot. A heart event puts the place card behind her bust (the backdrop); the other scenes stay plain until place art exists. A tap on the line (or on her bust) goes on; a heart event's line is typed out, three lines at most, as before.
  • Who's on the stage: the speaker; narration and thoughts show no one, except in a heart event, where she stays on the stage with the face of her last line. At a choice the last one to speak stays; meeting her ends on her smiling ("Welcome, ‹her name›").
  • Her bust is her portrait in the outfit the scene calls for, with the line's face over it:
    • a line's face is its emote; a line without one shows neutral, the portrait's own face;
    • only the face changes from line to line: the portrait stays put while the new face fades in over the last (140 ms; at once with reduced motion), and neutral fades the last face away; until a new face has loaded, the last one stays;
    • the next line's portrait and face load while this line shows.
  • Without her portraits she shows the blank bust: the silhouette of an Uma in the outfit the scene calls for, with a face for every one of the 22 expressions, so her face changes line by line like a portrait's (the blank bust). A role no one plays shows its lettered disc.

Her portraits

Master data's private dialogue-portraits family holds them, keyed by her character id: a portrait per outfit (525 × 700, her face neutral), with where the face sits on it, and a face per expression (224 × 224, with alpha), drawn to sit over any of her portraits. The game draws the face over the portrait at that portrait's face position, unscaled, in straight alpha. Her portraits load the first time she's on a stage, then each picture by its path: checked against the release and cached on the device by its hash, so a scene plays offline once seen. Three Umas have portraits so far, each with her outfits and her faces; the next follow in the Uma Ranker's order. An outfit or a face not drawn for her yet falls back (below), so a story can name one before its art exists.

Outfits by context

What she wears is the scene's own outfit when the story names one (an event's, a pair level's, a household scene's or the finale's); else the moment's, by the first rule that holds; else the day's season. An outfit not drawn for her shows her first in the art's order: spring.

The heart events’ moments: the wedding rung, a beat whose together is work, a picnic date, and sea-pier in summer. A household scene is a dinner and the finale a festival night; the calendar’s occasion scenes hand in New Year’s, the spring festival and festival night; the other moments are the evenings’ to hand in.

The blank bust

Every Uma without portraits plays every scene on the blank bust, drawn in the web app's code (dialogue/blank.tsx), so it needs no release and is replaced the moment her portraits are published.

  • The fallback order on a stage: her portraits, else the blank bust, else (a role no one plays) the role's lettered disc. The blank bust comes before her master-data bust because a scene's point is her face changing line by line, and the master-data bust has no faces. Her master-data bust stays where it always was, on cards and sheets; her small face everywhere else is her picture.
  • The base: a soft silhouette of an Uma (her ears, her long hair, her shoulders) at a portrait's size (525 × 700), in the scene's outfit by outfits by context: each of the 12 has its colours and its mark (the kimono's crossed collar and gold obi, the wedding dress's veil and gold flowers, the wedding night outfit's soft robe with blush lace, work clothes' overalls, pajamas' dots, and so on).
  • Her tint: her hair is her theme colour from master data's character set, softened toward a hair tone; her eyes and her ear ribbon take her second colour. Without one (a stand-in), a tone picked by her id, the same every time.
  • The face: a simple face for each of the 22 expressions (224 × 224), laid over the base at its face position (150, 188), exactly as a portrait's face is, and swapped with the same 140 ms fade (at once with reduced motion). All 22 are drawn, so an emote shows as written; an unknown one shows neutral.

| Expression | Her eyes | Her mouth | Its marks | |---|---|---|---| | neutral | Open | A small smile | — | | happy | Smiling arcs | A smile | A light blush | | laughing | Squeezed shut | Open, laughing | A light blush | | surprised | Wide | A small “o” | Brows high | | thinking | Looking up | To one side | Thought dots, one brow up | | sad | Drooping | A frown | Worried brows | | angry | Glaring | A frown | An anger mark | | worried | Open | Wavy | A sweat drop | | determined | Set | Firm | A sparkle | | pouting | Half-lidded | Pursed | A puffed cheek, a light blush | | shy | Looking down | A small smile | A deep blush | | tender | Softly closed | A smile | A light blush | | flustered | Wide | Wavy | A deep blush, blush lines | | loving | Smiling arcs | A smile | A deep blush, a heart | | tearful-joy | Smiling arcs | Open, smiling | Tears | | sleepy | Closed | A yawn | Z's | | crying | Shut tight | Open, sobbing | Streams of tears | | teasing | A wink | A cat's smile | A light blush | | smug | Half-lidded | A smirk | One brow up | | embarrassed | Squeezed shut | Wavy | A deep blush, blush lines, a sweat drop | | startled | Wide, small pupils | A big “O” | Alarm lines | | dreamy | Half-lidded, looking up | A smile | Sparkles, a light blush |

  • Her place holds while her portraits load, so a pilot's bust never flashes blank before her portrait.
  • The blank profile is the same head and shoulders, small: her picture while she has neither portraits nor a face in master data.

Her picture

Wherever an Uma's face shows small, as an avatar, it's her picture, in a rounded square. One avatar shows it everywhere: the town's map, who's here and its keepers, People in town, the calendar's cells and its day, the Hearts list, her profile's heading and its ties, the roster's cards, the work board, the Dorm and its pickers, Tonight, the evenings, the household, the farm map's houses, a scene's guests, the pair talks and the memories. Her picture is the first she has of:

  1. Her profile picture in today's season's outfit: uma-platform's 192 px square cut from her portrait, drawn whole. Three Umas have portraits so far, each with a profile picture per outfit.
  2. A crop of her portrait base, cut in place, for a release made before profile pictures (below).
  3. Master data's face for her: her outfit's face, or one of the town's people's character face. It shows as it always did, in the square instead of the circle. These faces are square (192 px, a character's 128 px), so they fill it exactly, neither cropped nor letterboxed.
  4. The blank profile in the season's colours (below), when master data has no face for her either (a stand-in).

│◆    ╱╲    ╱╲     │  a badge in a corner sits over her hair, her ears,
│    ╭────────╮    │  her shoulders or the backdrop, never her face

  • The avatar: a rounded square at the sizes the lists had (20 to 72 pt), its corners 18% of its side at any size, never a circle, with a hairline edge. The badges and counts a list puts on her face (her favourite line's mark, her best talent, her level, a ♥ or a ✓) keep their corners, 4 pt in, over the picture's edge and never clipped by it.
  • Her profile picture: her head and shoulders in her portrait's outfit for today's season (spring, summer, autumn's fall or winter), her calm face, the base's own. Master data's dialogue-portraits carries one per base (uma-data 1.9.0, uma-platform's P180): a 192 × 192 WebP of about 10 KB, cut from a square about 1.5 faces wide (331 to 336 px of a 525 × 700 base), her face centred and kept out of the corners at 28% of the side. An outfit without one shows her first in Farm Life's order that has one, spring first. On the next season's first morning every picture changes outfit.
  • Light: an avatar loads only her profile picture, never her 300 KB base. It's checked against the release and cached on the device by its hash, as a scene's pictures are. A list loads nothing of her portraits for an Uma without them (the art index says so before anything is fetched): she shows master data's face, as before.
  • A release made before profile pictures: the avatar cuts the same head and shoulders from her season's base in place (spring when that one isn't drawn): the base her scenes load, placed and scaled so a square fills the avatar, with no new file and no canvas. The square is 1.6 faces wide (358 px), centred across her face, her face's middle 46% down it, kept inside the base.
  • One seam: a single function picks her picture's source (pictureSource, in dialogue/picture.ts): her profile picture when the release has one, else her base, cut in place.
  • While her portraits are read, her square stays empty, so a pilot's picture never flashes another first; master data's face, too, leaves it empty until it has loaded.
  • Without portraits or a face she shows the blank profile, drawn in code: the blank bust's head and shoulders in the season's outfit, on the season's colour (the date chip's) washed light, her hair her theme colour's (else a tone by her id), and her letters ("S1") over her shoulders in ink, or white on winter's navy. Her letters come from her name.

| Season | Her picture's outfit | The blank profile's backdrop | Its outfit | |---|---|---|---| | Spring | spring | Pale green | Green, a pink collar | | Summer | summer, else spring | Pale gold | Sky blue, a white collar | | Autumn | fall, else spring | Pale orange | Orange, a russet collar | | Winter | winter, else spring | Pale blue | Navy, a white collar |

  • uma-kit's own pickers (the Dorm's) show the same: master data's face as before for an Uma without portraits, and her picture for one with them.
  • Her bust stays master data's on the cards and sheets that show a bust; a bust that hasn't loaded shows her picture until it has.

Her illustrations

At her big moments the scene opens onto a full-screen illustration. Master data's private illustrations family holds them, keyed by her character id: her scenes in the art's order, each with its title, its outfit and its moment (1672 × 941, from Uma Character Art's Farm Life universe). The first Uma's nine are published. An Uma without any plays every scene on her bust and face, exactly as before.

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

  • How it shows: the picture fills the whole screen in landscape, behind the title line (light over a soft shade) and the text box. The text box sits over the picture's lower edge: dark translucent glass, up to 46 rem wide, her name in rose, her line in white, narration and thoughts in italics. At a choice the line before it and the options sit in the same box, the options side by side where they fit. No bust while it's up.
  • A tap goes on as usual: on the line (and in the scenes where a tap on her bust goes on, a tap on the picture). Skip, Auto and Log work as before; a play's sheet sits over the picture.
  • It fades in as it comes up, from one picture to the next, and out as it goes down (450 ms); with reduced motion it simply swaps. While her picture loads the screen holds a dark ground, so her bust never flashes before it; a picture that can't load (offline, and never seen on this phone) shows her bust.
  • Each loads only when its scene starts: her manifest the first time a scene of hers cues one (or her gallery opens), then every picture the scene can show, checked against the release and cached on the phone by its hash, so a scene plays offline once seen. Nothing is fetched for an Uma the art index doesn't list.
  • Tender, never explicit: the owner approved every scene, and they show as published.
  • The scene ends on her bust: the meeting's Welcome card, an event's card and the stage-ups show her bust as before; the picture fades out.
  • Which, and when. The story names one with a step in her route's events, { "illustration": "<her scene's id>" }: up from there until the next such step (none takes it down) or the scene's end (the story pack's steps). A scene that names one plays only its own. Every other scene of hers plays the default mapping, each moment showing her first scene for it:

Her gallery

  • Unlocked for good: each illustration shown in a scene joins her gallery, once, kept in the save (gallery: her scenes' ids in the order first seen). A Memory replayed counts too: you've seen it.
  • Her profile's Gallery says "3 of 9" with Open › (?gallery=<id>), for an Uma with illustrations only.
  • The gallery opens full screen on a dark page: her name, "3 of 9" and ✕; every scene of hers in the art's order, three to a row (two on a narrow phone), each a 16:9 tile with its title under it. A seen one shows its picture; the rest show a locked silhouette (an Uma's head and shoulders on a dark tile, with a lock) and their titles, muted.
  • A tap on a seen one opens it full screen: the whole picture on black, its title and "1 / 3" at the foot, ‹ › (or a swipe, or the arrow keys) to her other seen ones, ✕ (or Escape) back to the grid.
  • Titles and pictures are master data's, read at runtime; a seen one's picture comes from the phone's cache.

The cast and the story pack

Everyone you've met is one of the cast: one character each, keyed by her character id everywhere the save names an Uma (the roster, her route, the Dorm, work, bonds, Memories and meeting scenes). She plays one outfit, is met when you introduce yourself, and has a route of her own. One you meet whom the pack doesn't hold joins the cast bare, on the default story.

What the pack holds

The pack is a folder of JSON files, kept out of git. It's a menu, not a checklist: every part is optional, any number of characters is fine, in any order, and nothing is counted. The story pack's menu lists every field the game reads, what it does in play, and what plays when it's missing.

FileHoldsWithout it
CastThe cast, in its order: each character's id (a sister's s<outfitId>, with her name) and, optionally, her outfit, calling, birthday, favourite spot, her home, job and routine, and chapters; the retired welcome list and trigger to meet are still readNo characters
TiesEach tie: two characters (a sister, or one of the town's people, among them) and, optionally, its kind, a strength of 1–3 and a lineNo ties
Her bible (one per Uma)Optionally her favourite line, gifts (loved, liked, disliked, any number each), her route's beats, chat sets, town lines, keepsake, pet name, wedding places and wishes. Her profile texts, season and festival are the story's ownHer calling's line (else her stats'), the default story's tastes (her line's favourite good loved) and talks, the game's town lines
Her route (one per Uma)Her scripted events, by id (the rungs, her life's events, her occasions, the evenings' scenes), any of themEach plays the default story's, which pays as a written one does
A pair's conversationsConversations at bond levels 1–5 for a tie's two UmasThe game's two-line card at each level
The householdHousehold scenes at 5, 10 and 15 married, house lines and the finale, every part optionalThe game's short cards; house lines from her temperament
The openingThe start's lines, any number: said, narrated, thought, pauses and the title line's placeThe game's two lines
The townThe town's people (each an Uma of master data's in a role), its six spots (name, line, keeper), and the homes, workplaces and spots it adds to the mapMaster data fills the roles; the game's own six spots and four shops and offices
The worldThe town's, the farm's and the region's names, the farm's history, the festivals, each place's name and line, a line for each season, and Town notes (below)The game's own words: its five festivals, its places' names and lines, and no Town notes

One reader, two kinds of findings. The game, the release and the Farm Story Sandbox read a pack with one reader (readStoryPack):

WhatWhat happens
An errorOnly what the game can't read: a file that isn't JSON or isn't the shape the game reads, a value the game can't use where it reads it, or a line longer than its screenIt skips the one part it's in (a line, a field, a beat, an event, a bond level, a tie, a character, at most a file); the rest plays
A noteEverything else: what isn't written yet, an unusual count, a bible and the tag sheet disagreeingNothing: notes block nothing

The game's backbone stays as built, and the story writes any of it: her arrival ("event zero"), Friendship 1–10, her confession, Romance 1–10, the proposal, the wedding and Spouse 1, 3 and 5 are the 27 rungs the game plays an event at. A beat says, each optional:

  • its setup: her full bar only, or one thing the player controls: she works an activity, a named good is in the Warehouse, or her bond with a named Uma reaches a level 1–5. Never an hour, the weather, a season or a festival;
  • what you do together: talk, work an activity, one of the ten date games (fishing, cooking, stargazing, footrace, dance, picnic, market, boat, walk, crafting), give a named good, or help another cast Uma;
  • its caption (the Memory's line), its hint (what her profile says while it's next), its place, and an unlock: an item, a place or a perk in words.

A scripted event is a list of steps: a line said by a cast Uma or one of the town's people, by her id or her role's (with an optional emote, one of the 22 expressions), narration, a thought, a pause, a change of place, a flag, a branch on a flag, a choice, or a play. The event may name its own outfit (outfits by context).

  • A choice has any number of options, each with its text, an optional tone (warm, playful or honest) and an authored gain that ranks the options. An option may cost a count of a good, reveal one of her gift tastes, or carry steps that follow it.
  • The usual sizes, a note when an event leaves them, never a refusal: a heart event has 1–2 choice points and at most one play on every path; the confession, the proposal and the wedding 1–3 choice points and at most three plays; a choice two or three options.
  • The confession's last choice point holds one Yes and one Not yet. An option there without an answer counts as Yes; answers anywhere else are ignored (a note).
  • Her arrival usually has one choice point and no play. Written with none it pays +50 alone; with more, the first pays.

Every line is within its screen's length (an error otherwise) and, as notes, follows two rules: {farmer} is the only placeholder, and nothing reads as school. The protagonist is a man (the owner, 2026-11-14): a line may say he, her husband, the groom, and no word about him is a note any more. A flag's name is one the save can keep (lowercase letters, digits and - . : _), and an option's cost and reveal name goods the game has: the save keeps them, so each is an error otherwise.

Her events past the rungs: a route may also write her life's events (wedding-night, moving-in, s2, s4), her occasions (birthday, new-year and the five festivals by id) and the evenings' scenes (hangout-<kind>, date-<kind>, dinner-in-town, a-night-in). Each replaces the default story's scene of that id; they pay nothing, so their choices are wording only, and the usual sizes don't apply. Any other id is a note, kept for the story.

Her chapters, and the retired welcome list and trigger

The cast file also gives her chapters, optional and acting now (her chapters), and two things that are retired, still read and acting on nothing:

ItemWhat it isWithout it
Her chapters (chapters)Their titles, which of her rungs each holds, and an optional gate from the trigger menu (the farm's kinds, a season or a festival)Four: "Getting to know her" (her arrival, F1–F10, the confession), "Together" (R1–R10, the proposal), "The wedding", "Married life" (S1, S3, S5)
The welcome list (welcome)Retired: nobody comes to the Gate. Read, noted, and acting on nothing—
Her trigger to meet (meet)Retired: you meet her by introducing yourself in town. Read, noted once, and acting on nothing—

The domain reads her chapters through chaptersOf, with its default; herChapters lays them along her route. welcomeOf and meetOf still read the retired two, for the Story Sandbox.

Her life in town

The cast file's home, job and routine say where she is, when; town.json's locations add the homes and workplaces they name. All optional; the story pack's menu has the exact shape, and the town how they place her: her routine's first stop that holds today, else her job on its days, else her home; on a day none holds, at home off the map. With none of them written, she keeps the game's default schedule (her favourite spot every third day of each season). They're what her profile's "Where to find her" and the calendar's "In town" show.

The world in the pack

The pack's world.json puts the world the story builds on screen; every part is optional, and anything missing keeps the game's own words (the story pack's menu has its exact shape). The domain reads it through the placeholders' lookups: worldNamesOf, historyOf, festivalsOf, storyDaysOf, placeWordsOf, seasonLineOf and townNotesOf.

PartIn playWithout it
The town's nameThe Town on the map and its screen's title, the opening's title line, the Gate's "Meet everyone else in Fernwick", the Diary's "The farm""Town" on the map and its screen; "in town"
The farm's nameThe opening's title line, the Diary's "The farm""The farm"; the opening says "Day 1"
The region's nameThe Diary's "The farm", after the townNone
The farm's historyThe Diary's "The farm" pageNone
The festivalsRenamed, moved or added: the calendar, the date chip, the morning, festival night, festival triggers and gates (festivals)The game's five
The story's daysIts holidays and minor days, on the calendar with their lines, and the morning's "Market day tomorrow" and "today" (the calendar screen)None
A place's name and lineThe map, its quick look, its Build cardThe game's own
A season's lineUnder "Spring begins" on the season's first morning"Spring begins" alone
Town notesThe Diary's Town notes, filling as you play; the morning names the night's (below)None

Town notes

Short pieces of the world that unlock as you play. Each unlocks once and stays.

 world.json's notes ──► no unlock ──────────────────────────────► known from the start
                    └─► an unlock from the menu ──► it holds at an introduction, a talk, a gift ──► "Town note · title ›" there
                                                    └─ or at night (step 8) ─────────────────► the morning names it
                                                                         both ──► the Diary's Town notes, kept
UnlockHoldsChecked
NoneFrom the start—
event (<characterId>:arrival is meeting her)That event has playedAn introduction, a talk or a gift in town, and every night
spotAn introduction or a talk with anyone at that town spot: a visitA talk in town
seasonAll that seasonAn introduction, a talk or a gift in town, and every night
festivalThat festival's day, as the story puts itAn introduction, a talk or a gift in town, and every night
farm-rank, built, shippedWhat the farm holdsAn introduction, a talk or a gift in town, and every night
  • The night checks after meeting her (step 8, unlockTownNotes) on the morning's date, so a season's note comes with its first morning, a festival's with its morning, and one for meeting someone the night after her meeting scene.
  • The save keeps only the ids unlocked, in the order they unlocked (diary.notes); the words are the pack's. A note with no unlock never enters the save. A note the pack drops leaves the list; one it brings back returns.
  • The Diary lists the notes you know in the pack's order, each a tap to read; the morning names the night's in New today, the first by its title ("title +2 more"), tagged Town note; an introduction, a talk or a gift that unlocks one says "Town note · title ›" there and then.

The pack at runtime

The game holds the cast, the ties, each bible by character id, the event ids each route scripts, the bond levels each tie's pair has a conversation at, the household, the opening, the town and the world. Routes and pairs' conversations load on demand, the first time they're needed.

  • Read, never refused. Every part is read on its own: a part with an error is skipped and the rest plays, so a pack never stops the game.
  • Never waited on. Every read of the pack goes through one lookup, the pack's or the default (story/placeholders.ts, the default story's home), so a part the pack doesn't write plays the game's own: an unscripted rung plays the default story's event, a pair's unwritten level its two lines, a household beat its one line. Nothing is gated on the story. The next section lists every default.
  • The save holds ids only: her character id, rung ids, flags, counts. Never a line of the story.

One outfit each, and the resolver

  • Her outfit is the cast file's: the game never chooses one over it. Her bust, talents and temperament come from that outfit; her name is her character's; her small face is her picture: her portraits' crop, else that outfit's face. Talents aren't saved, so a changed outfit shows on the next boot. Without one, she plays her default outfit from master data: her first original outfit (no variant, the lowest id), else her lowest. (What she wears in a scene is her portraits' outfit, by context: a different thing.)
  • One resolver turns a roster id into the outfit it plays: the cast's outfit, else master data's default for her, else the id itself for someone master data doesn't hold. The app sees master data through it, so faces, busts, names, talents, temperaments and pickers all follow the cast with nothing else to change.
  • A sister (s<outfitId>, the Ranker's) is an outfit of an Uma, presented as a character of her own: she plays that outfit (its face, bust, talents and temperament), and her name is the pack's cast entry's name, never in git (without one, her elder's name as her sister). She lives in town and is met like anyone; the roster takes her id.
  • Anyone in town meets master data through the same resolver: one of the town's people, or any other character, plays her default outfit; a role, the Uma who plays it. A character with no outfit shows master data's character face, else her blank profile.
  • A character master data doesn't hold shows her blank profile (the silhouette of an Uma in the season's colours, with her name's first letter and its number, "S1") and reads D talents in every activity. The stand-ins are named "Stand-in 1" to "Stand-in 3"; any other unknown character is "An Uma".
  • Everyone on the farm stays in the cast. An Uma on the roster the pack doesn't hold (one a release dropped, or one from an older story) joins the cast bare as the save comes in, at its end, and plays on the default story: her name and face from master data's default outfit for her, or her blank profile.

The stand-in pack

The repo carries an invented stand-in pack in the private pack's layout, so the romance is built and tested without a real character, name or line. It's the tests' only: no production build holds it, and the dev server plays no pack unless a release is there (pnpm story:release --stand-in writes one). Its first Uma, a farmer, has her whole route and every optional field; the forager and the fisher have only their arrival scenes and no bible, so the default story and the calling's line are exercised. Its opening is four invented lines, three narrated and then the farmer's thought. None of the three outfits is in master data, so all three show their blank profiles. The three stand-ins:

CharacterOutfitCallingFavourite lineBibleScripted
9000001900000101farmingfarmingYes27 of 27
9000002900000201foragingforagingNo (her calling stands in)1 of 27
9000003900000301fishingfishingNo (her calling stands in)1 of 27

How the app gets the pack

  • The release reads the private pack with the game's own reader and releases any pack, partial or not: it lists what it skipped (the errors) and the notes, and refuses only when there's no pack folder. It holds the pack as the game reads it, every broken part already skipped and every other field kept, and always a cast and a ties file (empty when the pack has none). It writes each file minified, named with the first 8 hex of its SHA-256 (story/<name>.<hash8>.json), and an index, story.json: every file with its path, size and hash, the event ids each route scripts, and the bond levels each pair holds. The same pack gives the same release.
  • The ship (pnpm story:ship, run where the story is written) checks the pack, builds the release, publishes it and backs the drafts up. Every ship is numbered (one more than the release the bucket held, or 1), dated, and has a note saying what's new (one line, 160 characters at most). The publish uploads the hash-named files first and the index last, to a private bucket. Old files stay, so a phone holding an older index still reads it, and a rollback publishes an earlier index again under a new number, its note "Rolled back to n". A pack the bucket already plays isn't published again.
  • farm-api serves it to the owner only: the index fresh on every read, the hash-named files cached for good, and nothing else (404).
  • On the phone, the boot reads the index, then the cast, the ties, the bibles, the household, the opening, the town and the world; each route and pair loads when it's first needed. Every file is checked against the index's hash and kept in the content cache, so a release's file downloads once. The boot and the night wait for the pack, as they wait for master data.
  • The last good index stays on the phone. When the index can't be read (offline, or the server failing), the boot plays the last version kept. The phone reads each file with the same reader, so a broken part is skipped there too. A release that can't load (no index and none kept, a file whose hash doesn't match, a route or pair that isn't what the index lists) stops the boot with Try again.
  • No pack published plays no pack: when farm-api serves none at all (a 404 for the index), the game plays with no pack, on the default story: master data's first five are the cast, so a fresh farm opens and plays whole. The boot waits for master data as well as the index, since the default story reads it.
  • "New story arrived." A fresh index whose number isn't the one the phone kept last is a new release. Once its pack has loaded, the phone keeps its number (farm.story-news, a few bytes) until the next night, which opens the morning's New today with "New story arrived · its note", tagged New; a tap opens the People page. It shows once a release on each phone; a release that arrives before the night replaces the one waiting. The save keeps the line's number only, never the note: the card reads the note from the release the game plays, and says "New story arrived" alone for another one. A new farm starts with none waiting, since its opening plays the story. An offline boot on the kept index, and a release published unnumbered (story:publish), say nothing.
  • Account's Story row shows the release the game plays: "Release 4, 14 Nov 2026" (its number and the day it shipped; an unnumbered one by its id and build date), or "No story yet" when none plays.

What plays without the story

The game plays whole with no story at all. Wherever it would read the pack, it plays the default story: written once for the game, warm and short, adults only, tender and never explicit, with her name, her favourite good and her line's work filled in at run time and nobody named. The protagonist is a man: it may say he, her husband, the groom. So every Uma plays her whole story from day one, from meeting her to married life, and nothing is blank. It reads as the game's own voice, with no "placeholder" label in play; the Farm Story Sandbox marks, for each character, what's written and what's the default ("In the game" on her page). Her own story replaces it piece by piece: her bible's chat set replaces the talks for its stage, her route's event of an id replaces that event (a rung, the wedding night, an occasion, an evening's scene), her bible's gifts replace her tastes; anything her pack leaves out stays default. Every ladder, event, perk, evening, proposal, wedding and household runs end to end without one.

The default story (story/default-story.ts, loaded with the story pack, never in the initial JS):

  • Her stage, as her talks follow it, is the Uma loop's: acquaintance to Friendship 2; friend from Friendship 3 through the confession's wait (farmhand once you've invited her to work); sweetheart from the confession through the proposal's wait; engaged until the wedding; married after it.
  • The invitation to work plays as you invite her, after the action is spent: her route's invited-to-work, else the default's, at the farm gate in the day's outfit (the invitation to work).
  • The wedding night plays right after the wedding's card, before the Married stage-up: her route's wedding-night, else the default's, in the wedding night outfit on a night backdrop, tender and fading to morning. Then moving in, her route's moving-in, else the default's, as she moves into the room she took; one who waited for a room plays it from the morning's "moved into" line. They pay nothing and save nothing; Skip ends each.
  • The evenings' scenes play after each evening's play: her route's hangout-<kind> (a friend's), date-<kind> (a sweetheart's or a spouse's), dinner-in-town and a-night-in, else the default's (the evening).
  • The night's companion's greeting and the meals' lines are her bible's mornings and meals when written, else the default story's (morningGreetingsOf, mealLinesOf), drawn by the day (married life).
  • The occasions the calendar plays: New Year's from the second year, the Flower Show and the Fishing Derby's night (derby-night), each on its day with everyone you've met (occasion scenes).
  • Married life 2 and 4 come at night when her bar fills, with no Ready event: the morning's "reached overnight" line plays her route's s2 or s4, else the default's (short and tender), then her level-up card (the level-up cards).
  • What waits for its moment: the other occasions (her birthday, the Fishing Derby's day, the Cooking Contest, the Animal Show, the Harvest Cup): written and read through the same lookup (eventOf); they play when the festivals give them their moment.

  • No pack at all (none published, or a pack whose cast names nobody): the cast is master data's first five characters by id, each in her default outfit with her master-data name. The game's two lines open the farm, and everyone is met in town by introducing yourself.
  • Her line from master data is the work her stats suit best, by the talents' one rule: each activity reads one stat (Stamina farming, Guts the ranch, Power mining, Wit fishing, Speed foraging), and a tie goes to the first in that order. A line whose places aren't built yet waits as a written one does ("Opens when the Mine is built").
  • A heart event's default is her moment, her lines with their faces and your reply: the warmer reply pays +30, the other +10, and with no play the best reply is a Perfect, so a card pays +50, +30 and +30 at best, as a written heart event does. Her beat's setup, place, play and unlock still apply when her bible has them. The big rungs keep their shape: her confession's reply holds Yes and Not yet, the proposal follows the ring you chose, and the wedding's last choice is the vows.
  • Her meeting scene is the default story's, in town, where you introduce yourself: +50 and the reply's +30 or +10. A written one with no choice gets the game's hello, so every meeting pays as one with a choice does.
  • A tie's pair has a conversation at every bond level: the pack's where it writes one, else two short lines, one each. A pair the ties don't hold has none ("‹a› and ‹b› are closer now").
  • The household plays its scenes at 5, 10 and 15 married and the finale at 20 whatever the pack holds: the pack's open and close where written, else a line each; each spouse's beat, else her one line; the finale's dance.

The opening, the farmer's name, and meeting her

The farmer's name

The first screen on a new farm covers everything until it's entered: "What should they call you?", one field and Continue, which stays off until the name fits. The words under the field say why ("Enter a name", "Letters, spaces, ' and - only", "16 characters at most"). Entering it is the farm's first player change. Menu → Account → Your name renames the farmer under the same rule.

The rule, and where the name shows:

RuleDetail
CharactersLetters of any script (with their accents), spaces, apostrophes (' and ’) and hyphens
Length1–16 characters, after trimming and collapsing inner runs of spaces
DefaultNone: an empty field with a grey “Farmer”
Where it shows{farmer} in every line, filled when the line shows; the save holds the name, never story text

No pronouns are asked: the protagonist is a man (the owner, 2026-11-14), never drawn. Lines say "you", and may say he, her husband, the groom.

The opening

A new farm takes under a minute: your name, a few lines, and you're on the farm. Day 1 runs in this order:

 the name sheet ──► the opening's lines, one to a tap ──► Home, day 1: nobody met yet, everyone in town
                     the pack's opening.json, any length;
                     else the game's two
                     Skip from the first line ─────────────┘
  1. The name sheet. Nothing else plays while the farmer is unnamed.
  2. The opening's lines: the pack's opening.json (any number of steps: said, narrated, thought, pauses and the title line's place), else the game's two. {farmer} is filled as each shows. The title line names where you are when the world names it, "Day 1 · Thistle Farm, Fernwick" (the farm, the town, or either alone), until a place step names a place ("Day 1 · Gate"); else "Day 1".
  3. Its last tap, or Skip on any line from the first, reads it once and opens Home on day 1: two taps after the name for the game's own two. No picks, no card, no tutorial.
  4. Nobody comes to the Gate (the owner, 2026-11-14): there's no welcome week. Everyone is in town from day 1, and you go to the Umas you want to meet (meeting her).

Nothing is picked or drawn. The farm has no global chapters, so the opening is the farm's first morning, never a plot. A farm whose opening was already read never plays it again, and keeps everyone it has.

Meeting her

Every Uma is in town from day 1, and you meet her by introducing yourself: one of the day's actions, in town, where she is now. From then on she has the whole romance: her ladders, heart events, perks, evenings, the proposal, the wedding and married life, in her own chapters; she shows on the People page. There's no cap on how many you meet: the Dorm's beds cap only who lives on the farm.

 the farm ──► Go into town · 1 ──► walk to her (free) ──► her menu: Introduce yourself · 1 ──► met: her meeting scene
                                                                                                  │
                                                       she lives in town ◄─────────────────────────┘
                                                         └── Invite to the farm (her profile), a bed free ──► the Dorm
  • Introduce yourself is on her menu while you haven't met: you're in town today, she's out there now, and an action is left ("Go into town first.", "She isn't out in town now.", "No actions left today." otherwise). One of the town's people is never met: she keeps her role.
  • Met: she joins the roster that day (roster[id].joined) at Friendship 0 with 0 points and lives in town, where you find her on her days for a talk, a gift, her evening and her events; Invite to the farm on her profile moves her into a free Dorm bed at once, in front of you every day (the farm's work is the invitation to work's, wherever she lives). Each of her ties with someone met starts at its seed.
  • Her meeting scene plays at once, over the town, after the introduction.
  • Retired with the welcome week: the Gate's visitor one a morning, Say hello, her trigger to meet (talks on three days, a gift, a spot, a season, a festival, what the farm holds) and the night's meetings. The pack's welcome and meet are still read, and noted as retired. Her chapters' gates stay, on the same trigger menu.
  • A sister (s<outfitId>) is met like anyone: the roster takes her id.
  • Everyone on the roster counts as met, the day she joined the day you met her.

Her meeting scene

Her meeting scene is her route's arrival scene, and plays as soon as you introduce yourself (above), after the opening and the ring bench's scene, in the order you met them (by day, then the cast's order). The scene player shows her lines with her bust (her portrait in the scene's own outfit, else the day's season, her face the line's emote; else the blank bust in that outfit with the same face, or a role's disc) and {farmer} filled, narration, thoughts in italics, a title line ("Meeting Stand-in 1 · Gate"), pauses, flags (wording only) and her choice: usually one; written with none she pays +50 alone, and with more the first pays.

  • Each option shows its gain on the button before the tap.
  • Skip jumps to the choice, never past it; after the choice, to the end. So every meeting pays.
  • With her illustrations it opens on her meeting's picture, full screen, its lines and her choice over it (her illustrations).
  • It ends on her bust, smiling, "♥ +80" (or +60), where she lives ("She lives in town: find her there.", or "She lives on the farm." for one already there) and Welcome, ‹her name›, and the town stays where it was.

What it pays into her Friendship 1 bar:

Her meeting scenePoints into her bar
Played or skipped to its end+50
Its choice: the option with the highest authored gain (each one tied at the top)+30
Its choice: any other option+10
Written with no choiceThe game’s hello is added, so its choice pays as above
Written with more than one choiceThe first pays; the rest are words only

It pays once and is stamped once (arrivals). A route without her arrival scene plays the default story's, its reply worth +30 or +10, in town, where you introduce yourself ("You introduce yourself, and the time slips by."). A written one with no choice ends on the game's hello. Any pack can leave one unwritten: the release notes it, and the default plays.

Her favourite line and tastes

  • Her favourite line is her bible's, else her calling's (a quarry calling is mining, at the Quarry), else her line from master data: the work her outfit's stats suit best. It is never master data's running strategy. It gives her ×1.2 rank points in that activity, the mark on her face card, +10 a night when she works it, and every perk that works "at her line". Her anniversary item, morning items, night chores, signature dish, mentoring and the place a Walk favours all read it.
  • Her line's places:
Her lineHer places
FarmingThe Fields (a block she tends)
ForagingThe forest and the Sawmill
FishingThe Fishery and the Smokehouse
MiningThe Mine and the Smelter; with a quarry calling, the Quarry and the Cutter
RanchThe Coop and the Barn
CookingThe Kitchen
ServingThe Restaurant
ForgingThe Forge
  • Her tastes are her bible's: loved, liked and disliked goods, any number of each. Everything else is neutral, and a good not in the game yet counts as neutral. An Uma whose bible names no taste has the default story's: her favourite good loved (by her line: a Carrot for farming, a Trout for fishing, the full list in what plays without the story), every other good neutral. Her favourite good is what the default story's lines call {good}. Every default can be had in Spring of year 1, with nothing built: the woods' Spring goods, the pond's Spring fish, or a crop of the shop's first Spring seeds (a test checks it against the tables).
  • Her tastes show in steps: her loved good from Friendship 1; any good once you've given it, or once an event's option or a Choose play has revealed it; every taste from Friendship 7.

The Uma loop

From meeting her to married life, in the owner's stages. Her points, events, plays, perks, the ring and the wedding are her route as built; the loop names the stage she's at, says what each opens, and adds its own acts: the invitation to work, the evenings at two actions, moving in, the household's meals and the night's companion (romance/loop.ts).

 meet ─► acquaintance ─► friend ─────► farmhand ─────► sweetheart ─► engaged ─► married
 (town)  talks, gifts    hangouts · 2   invited to      dates · 2     the        the wedding night, moving in;
         (1 each)        invite to      work · 1        Dinner in     wedding    meals · 1; the night's companion,
                         work · 1       (on the farm    town (R3),               who greets you in the morning
                                        by day)         A night in (R6)

The stages

StageHow you reach itWhat it opens
AcquaintanceMet (you introduced yourself), Friendship 0–2Talks and gifts, an action each
FriendFriendship 3 and upHangouts (2 actions); the invitation to work (1 action)
FarmhandA friend you’ve invited to workShe’s on the farm by day, in front of you every day, and works it part-time (the farmhands’ work); her farmhand talks
SweetheartFrom her confession’s yes (Romance 0)Dates (2 actions) from Romance 1; Dinner in town from Romance 3, A night in from Romance 6
EngagedAfter the proposalThe wedding
MarriedAfter the weddingThe wedding night and moving in; the household’s meals; the night’s companion
  • Her stage shows on her profile, by her level ("Farmhand" beside "Friend · F5"), and on her menu in town ("Farmhand · F5"). It's read from her ladders and one flag, never stored on its own: her ladder's stage, and roster[id].farmhand, the day you invited her to work.
  • A farmhand is a farmhand while she's a friend; from her confession on, her ladder's stage shows, and she keeps working the farm. Her talks follow her stage (what plays without the story).
  • Hangouts open at Friend, Friendship 3, her "Hangouts" perk; dates at Romance 1, her "Dates" perk. A sweetheart at Romance 0 (the day after her confession's yes) still has hangouts.

The invitation to work

  • Invite her to work · 1, on her menu in town or her profile, once she's a friend and where you meet her; once.
  • She's a farmhand from today and keeps her life in town (the Dorm is only where she sleeps if you invite her to the farm), but she's on the farm by day, after her part-time job: in front of you every day for talks, gifts and her evening. From tonight she works the tasks you set her on the work board, and her aptitudes grow (farmhands). The flag is also her stage and her talks.
  • Her scene plays as soon as the action is spent, over her menu or her profile (?life=invited-to-work:<id>): her route's invited-to-work, else the default story's, a short scene at the farm gate in the day's outfit (her everyday one) unless hers names another. It pays nothing and saves nothing; Skip, or its end, closes it.

The household's meals

Married life
A mealBreakfast or Dinner: 1 action, each once a day, from your first wedding
Who eatsEveryone on the farm (the Dorm and the houses) and every farmhand
What a meal doesEveryone it feeds well fed for 2 days (today and tomorrow; a meal running longer is never shortened), and +10 to each (addLove, an anniversary’s ×2 applies); and a batch for the pantry, 4 meals from 2 goods of food by the plain rule, when the Warehouse holds them
Its sceneOne spouse’s meal line (draw(uid, day, 'meal:<meal>', 0)): her bible’s meals, else the default story’s, on the dialogue engine
The night’s companionAt Tonight, one spouse with a room: free. Her house is tonight’s house, so its morning is as built

  • Where: the homestead's sheet (a house's tap on the map), Meals: Breakfast · 1 and Dinner · 1, once each a day ("Breakfast ✓"), and how many are at the table.
  • A batch too: a meal is the farmer cooking for the household, so it puts four meals in the pantry from two goods of food by the plain rule, when the Warehouse holds them (food).
  • As you sit down, one spouse's line plays on the dialogue engine, in the day's outfit (?life=breakfast:<id>).
  • Well fed counts down by one each night, so a meal today still feeds everyone tomorrow (well fed).

The night's companion

  • Tonight with, on Tonight once anyone is married: one row a spouse with a room, her face, her name and her house, ☕ when her house's breakfast is on tomorrow, "9 nights" for a house you haven't slept in for 8 or more, and on a birthday-alone wisher's birthday "Her room · her birthday, just you two". The pick, else the night's default, is marked; a tap picks her at once, free. Her house is tonight's house, so its morning is as built (the night's house and its morning).
  • Tender, never shown: the night is the fade, nothing more.
  • In the morning she greets you: as the morning comes up, a short scene in pajamas on a morning backdrop, her greeting her bible's mornings, else the default story's for her temperament, drawn by the day (?life=morning:<id>). With her illustrations the morning's narration plays over companion, before her greeting on her bust. It pays nothing and saves nothing (a picture seen joins her gallery). The morning's first Hearts line, "☾ Morning with Name" (Play), plays it again.
  • The save: your pick is homestead.companion (with homestead.tonight her house) until the night, which notes her (love[id].night, the night she was last your companion) and clears it.

Her route

Stages and costs

One track per Uma, 26 rungs with an event, plus the spouse levels S2 and S4, which have no Ready event and come at night when their bar fills (the morning's line plays their scene):

 F1 F2 F3 F4 F5 F6 F7 F8 F9 F10   ◆    R1 R2 … R8 R9 R10   ◇      ◇       S1  S2  S3  S4  S5
 ─────── friendship ─────────  confession ── romance ─────  proposal wedding ──── spouse ─────
 200 240 280 … 560 (3,800)        0     200 … 560 (3,800)     0       0      600 600 600 600 600
 an event at every level          an event                   events: ring   events at S1, S3, S5

A friendship or romance heart costs 160 + 40 × its number; a spouse level costs 600:

Heart12345678910Ladder
Friendship or romance2002402803203604004404805205603,800
Cumulative2004407201,0401,4001,8002,2402,7203,2403,800
Spouse6006006006006003,000
A route: the three waiting rungs cost 010,600
  • A level is its event. Her bar fills, her next event turns Ready, and the level comes when it plays. The cost leaves her points and the rest stays banked.
  • The moves: Friendship 10 played leaves her waiting at her confession. Its Yes starts romance at Romance 0. Romance 10 leaves her waiting for the proposal; the proposal makes her engaged, waiting for the wedding; the wedding makes her a spouse at Spouse 0 and marks her wedding day. Spouse 5 completes the route.
  • The waiting rungs cost nothing. The confession, the proposal and the wedding are each Ready the day after the rung before; the proposal also needs a ring in the Warehouse.
  • Points never fall, and nothing is lost or locked. Past a full bar they're banked ("+120 banked") and carry into the next bar when the event plays. While a waiting rung waits, every point banks into the next ladder.
  • Points are whole: each pay rounds once, half up, after its factors. Once Spouse 5 has played nothing more is added.
  • Her level in words: "New friend" at F0, "Friend · F7", "Friend · F10" while her confession waits, "Close friend" after a Not yet, "Partner" at R0, "Partner · R4", "Partner · R10" while the proposal waits, "Engaged" while the wedding waits, "Spouse", "Spouse · S2".

The bar's colour carries the ladder:

LadderStagesBar

RomanceR0–R10, the proposal and the wedding while they waitrose #e0567a
SpouseS0–S5 (600 a level)gold #d9a227

Points from every source

Every number shows before or as it's earned: the buttons say it ("Chat · 1" with its +15, "Praise +20"), the gift picker shows each good's points, and a "+N" floats from her face (still, with reduced motion). Every act pays through one rule, which applies the day's factors and says what it paid. The acts, what each pays and how often:

  • The day's factors multiply once each: a gift ×3 on her birthday; on her anniversary every act ×2 except the evening, which takes its own ×1.5 instead.
  • Chat (tap her): +15, once a day each, on the farm or in town ("You've talked today."). Her line for the day is drawn by the account, the day and her id, so it's the same all day. From Friendship 1 her bible's chat set for her stage plays when it holds any: friend through her confession (Romance 0 included), partner from Romance 1 to the wedding, spouse after it. Anything else plays the default story's talks for her stage in the Uma loop and her temperament, her favourite good and her line's work in.
  • Gift: one good from the Warehouse at the stars you choose, once a day each, on the farm or in town ("One gift a day."; a seed is "That isn't a gift."). The good is used, and its taste is learnt. Stars multiply by 1 + 0.1 a star above ★1:

Loved, on her birthday (×3)180198216234252

Neutral, on her birthday (×3)3033363942

A disliked gift pays 0 and never takes points away. She says one of the default story’s lines for her taste (“{name} thanks you, a little puzzled.”).

  • The gift picker lists the Warehouse by family, each good at each star held, with its points for her today (the birthday's ×3 and the anniversary's ×2 included), or "?" where her taste for it isn't known.
  • Praise: she earns it for the day when her work that finished overnight made a ★3+ item, when she ranked up last night, or when a farmhand's harvest or haul tonight gave a ★3+ good. Assisting a Fine-or-better workshop play is a cause too, but nothing pays that cause yet. Praise +20 shows on her profile only while earned and unpaid, pays once, and both lists clear at night.
  • Her line: at night, +10 to each Uma who worked today at one of her line's places. One a night each; a day elsewhere pays nothing. Only farmhands work, so only they earn it.
  • A festival together: +50 to each Uma on a festival's team or entry, once a festival a year each. The rule is written, but no festival pays it yet.

Birthdays and anniversaries

Her birthday is master data's date for her character, read at runtime; a birthday in her cast entry wins where present. A real date maps onto the farm's 48-day year by quarters of its month: season day = 4 × (the month's place in its season, 0–2) + ⌈min(day, 31) ÷ 8⌉, held to 1–4.

MonthDays 1–89–1617–2425–31
MarchSpring 1Spring 2Spring 3Spring 4
AprilSpring 5Spring 6Spring 7Spring 8
MaySpring 9Spring 10Spring 11Spring 12
JuneSummer 1Summer 2Summer 3Summer 4
JulySummer 5Summer 6Summer 7Summer 8
AugustSummer 9Summer 10Summer 11Summer 12
SeptemberAutumn 1Autumn 2Autumn 3Autumn 4
OctoberAutumn 5Autumn 6Autumn 7Autumn 8
NovemberAutumn 9Autumn 10Autumn 11Autumn 12
DecemberWinter 1Winter 2Winter 3Winter 4
JanuaryWinter 5Winter 6Winter 7Winter 8
FebruaryWinter 9Winter 10Winter 11Winter 12
  • On her birthday a gift pays ×3 and an evening ×1.5, and the day gives one action for events. The morning says so the day before and on the day, and the calendar shows her face on it. Two Umas may share a birthday; a birthday on a festival day is both.
  • Her anniversary is her wedding's day of the year, from a year after the wedding on (every 48 days after it). That day every act pays ×2 (the evening ×1.5 instead), and that morning she leaves a ★5 item of her line in the Warehouse, on the farm or in town: a crop of the season for a farmer, a good of her foraging rank's deepest open area this season for a forager, a fish of her deepest open spot for a fisher, and a crop of the season for any line whose place isn't built yet, so an anniversary is never empty. The morning says so the day before and on the day, and the calendar shows her face on it.

Ready

Her next event is Ready when all four of these hold, wherever she lives: it plays from her menu in town, or from her profile, an action each:

#Ready needsNot met
1Her bar is full for the rung; a waiting rung (the confession, the proposal, the wedding) is Ready the day after the rung before, and the proposal needs a ring in the Warehousebar, day, ring
2She hasn’t played one of her events todaytoday
3Its one setup is met, if it has one: she works a named activity today (a running job there, or a tended growing block for farming), a named good is in the Warehouse at any stars, her bond with a named Uma has reached a levelsetup (a working setup is met every day, its place built or not: the farm gates her only through her chapters)
4Her chapter is open: when her next event is the first of her chapter, the chapter’s gate holds, if it has one (a place built, a Farm Rank, a good shipped, another’s event, a season or a festival)gate: “Opens when the Kitchen is built”

A setup is never an hour, the weather, a season or a festival; her chapter's gate may be a season or a festival (see her chapters). When she isn't Ready, the first of these in the way is the one shown:

OrderwaitsWhen
1doneHer route is complete (S5 has played)
2nightS2 or S4, bar full: no Ready event; the level comes at night, and the morning plays its scene; short of it, bar
3friendShe said “Not yet” at her confession: you ask her again from her profile
4gateHer next event opens her chapter, and its gate doesn’t hold yet
5barHer bar isn’t full for the rung
6dayA waiting rung, on the day the rung before played
7ringThe proposal, with no ring in the Warehouse
8todayShe played one of her events today
9setupIts day setup isn’t met today
  • A setup is her bible's beat's setup for the rung; an Uma without a bible has none. A working setup ("she works the Fishery today") is met every day, farmhand or not, and whether or not its place is built (the Restaurant, the Forge, the Kitchen, the Mine), so her event never waits on work she may not do and a route never dead-ends on a setup. The farm gates her only through her chapters' gates, which the story chooses: a place built or a Farm Rank there holds her chapter's first event, by name. A pack's beat that still names a chapter setup is noted and read as her bar alone: the farm has no global chapters, and a gate goes on one of her chapters.
  • Her bond setup reads bond hearts (50, 150, 300, 500 and 750 points). The proposal's ring is any ring in the Warehouse.
  • Ready is read live, never stored, since it changes by the day: her Hearts row, her profile, the morning's Ready lines, her pip on the map and the calendar's faces all read it as they show. The calendar reads a day ahead the same way, from the save as it stands with the date moved (the calendar screen).
  • Ready is hers, the action is yours: Ready never reads the day's actions, and playing her event costs one (the special day's event actions first; a day of ten actions).

The People page

People (/hearts, the rail's third page) is the Umas' page: one row an Uma you've met, on the farm or in town, two to a row where they fit, a filter row, and her profile on a tap. It has no badge. Its two views ride in the URL: Hearts (the default) and Work, the roster's album with her level in words ("F7", "R4", "S2") in each face's corner. /roster lands on the Work view.

  • A row: her face, her name, her chapter at a glance ("Ch. 2 · Together"; "Married life ✓" once her last has closed) and chips (✉ while her invitation stands, unanswered or said yes and not yet spent; In town), and at its end ● Ready in her stage's colour, which plays her event; a tap elsewhere opens her profile. Under it, her level in words, a filling heart, her ladder's hearts as a bar (ten for friendship and romance, five as a spouse: the levels played full, the next filling by points over its cost, the rest empty) and "points/cost". A waiting rung shows every heart full with its mark, ◆ her confession or ◇ the proposal and the wedding, and "Tomorrow" or "Ready".
  • The row's line: Ready, her rung and where it plays ("Ready · Romance 5 · the Pond at night"); a short bar for one who lives in town, "Lives in town: find her there"; a short bar, nothing more (the bar says it); otherwise what her next event waits for ("Friendship 3 · the Fields, when she works there", or her chapter's gate: "Friendship 7 · Opens when the Kitchen is built"); with none of these, a spouse's house ("Rose Cottage") or "Needs a room". Then "+N banked" (cut first on a narrow row), and today's ✓chat and ✓gift.
  • The sort: Ready first, then the least progress (her next rung's place on the route, then her points), then the cast's order.
  • The filters, one at a time, each with its count: Ready; Friends (friendship, and her confession's wait); Partners (romance, and the proposal's and the wedding's waits); Married; In town.
  • With no one met yet: "No one yet. Go into town and introduce yourself."
  • The header: "Married n/N", N everyone you've met (3 with the stand-ins); a household scene or the finale's chip while one is Ready ("⌂ Scene 1 ›", "⌂ Harvest ›"); and ☾ Evening with a mark a slot today, ○ free or ● spent, the evenings first and then festival night's.
  • The (i) has six lines: how her bar fills and banks; Ready and setups; one event a day; her chapters ("A chapter may wait for the farm first"); gifts and tastes; and the evening ("One evening a day with someone home, 2 actions: a hangout from Friendship 3, a date from Romance 1. Fine +60, Great +100, Perfect +150, ×1.5 in her favourite kind, and +20 when she asked you.").

Her profile

Her profile is a side panel over the page, opened from her row, a Work card, the morning's lines, the Dorm's list and the town. Each part shows only what exists; nothing says "Soon". One who lives in town has, under her face, Where to find her, then Lives in town and Invite to the farm while a bed is free (she moves in at once), else "No bed free." and The Dorm ›.
